# 02 — Session Log

Index of every completed task on `apps/web`. Newest first. One row per **task**,
not per session — if a session completes three tasks, three rows appear here.
The detail lives in the record each row links to.

Writing the record and indexing it here is mandatory the moment a task reaches
`done` (`rules/WEB-007-session-record-and-commits.md`). This app is transformed
by agents with no memory between sessions. This file is the memory.

| Date | Task | Record | Outcome |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| 2026-08-17 | T-149 · the coverage widget stops being two charts that say the same thing twice | [2026-08-17-T-149-coverage-widget-segmented-meter](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-149-coverage-widget-segmented-meter.md) | **Follow-up to T-147 — the recolour quieted the widget but not its shape.** `measure-coverage` drew a **2-slice donut** next to a **"Blocked by reason" bar that was always 100% wide** (`domainMax = the only count`, so a lone bar fills its track every time and carries zero information — the "clutter"). Mapbox is configured in dev so the real map loads, but the prop guards env without a token. **Found and fixed:** my first pass made the sections `h3` under the `h1` (a skip); promoted to `h2`. **Verified a real pointer click drives the selection** — a programmatic `.click()` did not fire React's controlled-radio `onChange` (synthetic-event quirk), but a real click selects the card with the acid-lime `--sqx-border-accent` border; the e2e uses `.check()` (real events). **Verified:** typecheck 0, typography PASSED (relocked 1287→1284), lint PASSED (relocked 7615→7599), v5 **71 unchanged** (the package effective-date `toISOString().slice` moved page→queries.ts, net zero), `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**. Temporary axe file removed and confirmed by a **404**; browser restored to dark/EN. **The live browser e2e was re-pointed, not left to break:** `verify-unregistered-establishment` (INSP-605, a real submit against dev+Supabase, runs in the full e2e suite) clicked `.sq-typecard` and `getByRole("button","Complaint received")`; both markups became radios, so its selectors moved to `getByRole("radiogroup",{name:/Report type/i}).getByRole("radio").first().check()` and `getByRole("radio",{name:"Complaint received"}).check()`. **Owed: light theme, 200% zoom, browser e2e.** **The establishments surface is now fully migrated (list + create form).** |

Param parsing and the href builder became pure functions in `params.ts`. **Two acid-lime contrast traps, caught by measuring, not by eye.** (1) The active tab used `--sqx-surface-accent` — a **soft dark-olive tint** (`#292b13` in dark), NOT the bright fill — with dark `inverse` text, giving **1.23:1**; fixed to the bright `--sqx-action-primary-bg` fill with its ink and `tone="inherit"`, now void ink (`rgb(8,9,10)`) on lime. (2) The card avatar had the same soft-surface+dark-text (a barely-visible initial, decorative so axe stayed silent) — moved to a neutral `--sqx-surface-sunken`. **`--sqx-surface-accent` is a background-behind-normal-text token, never a fill for inverse text** — the T-129 lesson, re-learned. **And a layout trap:** I copied home's `max-inline-size: var(--sqx-grid-min-lg)` (384px) but **omitted its `@media (min-width: 60em) { max-inline-size: none }` override**, so the card grid clamped to a 384px single column until I added the second line — the home pattern needs both. **Added the `filter` icon** (`SlidersHorizontal`), the sixth this `/field` sweep has needed; the drawer `✕` literal → the `dismiss` icon. **Verified:** typecheck 0, typography PASSED (relocked 1299→1287), lint PASSED (relocked 7669→7615), v5 **71** (was 72), `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**. Refactored to a single `async safeRead<T>() → {value, ok}` helper, `Promise.all` for the per-draft item counts instead of a mutating `for`-loop, and a derived `readFailed` — governed behaviour (server drafts render immediately, local drafts layer in once IndexedDB settles, unknown states render nothing rather than a guess) unchanged, and `field-offline-isolation`'s `localForUser` assertion still holds after its path was re-pointed to the new file. **`as unknown as VisitRow` is gone** — `queries.ts` narrows from `unknown`. **Added the `info` icon** (`lucide/Info`) for the two footnote panels — the fifth semantic name this `/field` sweep has needed (`create`/`elapsed` in T-138, `info` here). **Verified:** typecheck 0, typography PASSED (relocked 1306→1299), lint PASSED (**relocked 7720→7669, −51 — the six removed `let`s plus others**), v5 72 unchanged, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**. | 2026-08-17 | T-140 · `/field/my-tasks` migrated off the parallel design system | [2026-08-17-T-140-field-my-tasks-migration](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-140-field-my-tasks-migration.md) | **The second `/field` slice — a master/detail screen with a live Factory 360 dossier.** The route file went **611 lines → 16**, split into `features/field-my-tasks/{queries,rows,labels}.ts` and nine components under `components/sections/field-my-tasks/`, largest 187. **Headings went from five `h3`s and no `h1` to `1>2>2>2>2>2>2`** (one `h1`, one `main`), rendered type from nine ad-hoc sizes with weight 700 to `13·15·24` capped at 590, 3 raw `<svg>` → 0, and **103 in-code `tr(key,en,ar)` call sites → 0** with 95 keys moved to a new `field-my-tasks` namespace (Arabic lifted from the pairs). **axe: 0 violations across English/dark and Arabic/dark, on both the list-only and full-dossier renders**, best-practice rules included. **Two defects found only by measuring, both mirrors of T-138.** (1) GeoMap hard-codes its "Map unavailable" heading at `h4` — valid under the home page's `h3` card title (`h3→h4`), but an **`h2→h4` skip** here under the `h2` Visit Location section, which axe's `heading-order` caught. Fixed with an **additive** `unavailableHeadingLevel` prop on GeoMap (**default 4, so every other consumer is byte-for-byte unchanged**), passed `3` through `FieldLocationMap` (now a my-tasks-only component). (2) The Arabic render showed the risk pill as `35 · low` and the licence type as `industrial` **in English** — my first pass routed governed enums through a local status table that lacks `low`/`medium`/`high`, where the old code used the shared `enum.*` namespace (`low → منخفضة`); fixed by threading `messages.visits.enum` into the detail via `enumLabel`, exactly as T-138 does, while the **list's** operational-state labels stay in the new namespace because `scr-ipad-600` asserts them there. **`assignment-task-model.ts` and `connectivityPresentation` were reused unchanged** — pure governed filter/sort/offline logic with a passing contract, imported by `/field/visits` too; only the UI shell `AssignmentTaskBrowser` was deleted. **`design-foundation-contract` encoded a dead rule:** it asserted the authenticated tree holds **exactly one** `linear-gradient`, pinned to `my-tasks.module.css`, but WEB-009 §11's gradient budget is **zero** and the migrated sheet has none — the assertion was flipped to `toBe(0)` and the pinned-selector check dropped, so it now enforces the current law. **A TSDoc comment I added to GeoMap tripped the ratchet** (GeoMap is not under `components/saqeel/`, so no TSDoc exemption) and was removed — the prop name is self-documenting and the baseline held. **`normal` priority still renders a red `Priority: normal` pill** — pre-existing, outside the governed low/medium/high/urgent set, parked. **Verified:** typecheck 0, typography PASSED (relocked 1336→1306), lint PASSED (relocked 7812→7720), v5 **72** (was 75), `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**, and `scr-ipad-600`/`field-a11y-hardening`/`factory360-ipad-field` (run under a temporary in-repo config, removed after) all green. Temporary axe file removed and confirmed by a **404**; browser theme and locale restored to dark/EN as found. **Owed: light theme, 200% zoom, browser e2e.** **Deleted 480 lines** (`AssignmentTaskBrowser` 211, `TaskHeaderStatus` 73, `PrepareAssignmentAction` 42, old stylesheet 154); the route folder drops from 7 files to 3. **Parked:** `normal`-priority pill · GeoMap's hard-coded EN/AR strings · the header duplicating the AppShell. |
| 2026-08-17 | T-139 · "Revamp" out of the component names | [2026-08-17-T-139-revamp-rename](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-139-revamp-rename.md) | **Four components were named after the project phase that produced them, not what they render.** `git log --diff-filter=A` answers the question in one line — `b91b8905 feat(web): migrate Saqeel revamp shell and core workspaces` — so `RevampStrategicView` told a reader *when* the file was written, and once the revamp is the current state of the app the word carries no information at all. Renamed to `StrategicBoard`, `OperationalBoard`, `Factory360Portfolio` and `OperationsCenter`, with 4 importers and 4 specs re-pointed. **`Board`, not `View`, because the obvious names collide:** `DashboardView.tsx` **already exports its own `StrategicView` and `OperationalView`**, so the prefix had been doing real disambiguation work — `TS2440: Import declaration conflicts with local declaration` caught it on the first attempt, which is the whole argument for renaming through the compiler rather than through a find-and-replace. **`RevampFactoryRow` was deleted rather than renamed** — it was `export type RevampFactoryRow = FactoryRow;`, a pure re-export of a type `@/features/factories/portfolio` already exports, so the indirection went with the keyword. **What was deliberately left alone, and why the rule is not "remove the word everywhere":** the **`admin.revamp.*` i18n keys are DB-backed** — three migrations insert their Arabic, so renaming the keys in code alone would **silently drop the Arabic on `/admin/integrations` and `/admin/risk`**, making it a migration rather than a rename; `product-contract/**` (`SLR_SAQEEL_LOGIN_REVAMP_001.csv`, `TASK_SAQEEL_RESPONSIVE_REVAMP_001.yaml`, `CC-SAQEEL-RESPONSIVE-REVAMP-001.yaml`) carries **stable contract IDs** that `.claude/rules/governance.md` requires to stay stable across plans, code, tests, evidence and handoffs; migration filenames *are* the ledger entry; `design/final-cut/saqeel-revamp.html` is named as the structural design authority in `CLAUDE.md`; and the "supplied Revamp" comments and test titles refer to that artefact **by its actual name**, so renaming them would make the reference wrong. **The distinction: a filename should say what the thing is — but a governance artefact's name *is* its identifier.** **A finding surfaced by the collision, and parked:** `DashboardView.tsx`'s exported `StrategicView` (line 261) and `OperationalView` (line 527) each compute a requirement strip, `return` the delegating element, **and then continue with the entire pre-revamp implementation below the `return`** — marker projections, compliance breakdowns, headline id lists, all unreachable and all typechecking, which is exactly why nothing has caught it. **Verified:** typecheck 0, typography PASSED, lint PASSED, v5 75 unchanged, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**. **Both ratchet baselines are keyed by file path, so a rename reads as "7 new violations"**; relocked, and the totals prove it was neutral — typography **1336 across 278 entries** and eslint **7812 across 906 entries**, identical before and after, the same violations under new keys. A rename that had actually added anything would have moved those numbers. |
| 2026-08-17 | T-138 · the `/field` home migrated off the parallel design system | [2026-08-17-T-138-field-home-migration](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-138-field-home-migration.md) | **The first `/field` slice, closing the gap T-137 measured.** The route file went **677 lines → 12**, its logic split into `features/field-home/{queries,rows,labels}.ts` and eleven components under `components/sections/field-home/`, none over 136 lines. **The page had zero headings and now has a full outline** — `1>2>2>3>2>3>3>2>2>2>2`, one `h1`, one `main`, no skips — and **rendered type went from nine ad-hoc sizes with weight 700 (`11.5·12·12.5·13·13.5·14·14.5·16·19`, five below the 13px floor) to four on-scale sizes capped at 590 (`13·15·24·32`)**. **axe: 0 violations across English/dark, Arabic/dark and Arabic/light**, best-practice rules included. 15 raw `<svg>` → 0 via the icon registry (two names, `create` and `elapsed`, did not exist and were added — the sanctioned mechanism under rule 8); 66 inline `style={{}}` → 2 (a derived percentage and the segmented control's own custom properties); 33 legacy type classes → 0; 86 comment lines → 0. **The slice was bounded by the import graph, not the directory — the T-136 lesson applied before writing code rather than after.** `FieldHeader` has **34 importers** and `FieldHeaderSync` **4**, so rewriting either would have silently restyled 33 unmigrated field screens; the home route simply stops importing `FieldHeader` and renders its own header, and `FieldHeaderSync` is reused as-is with its 2 inline-font violations left attributed to it. `FieldHome`, `FieldMetricStrip` and `DailyBriefingCard` had **exactly one importer each** — this route — and were deleted at zero imports along with the old 194-line stylesheet, **523 lines** total, taking `components/field/` from 22 files to 19. **A local helper had institutionalised the banned copy pattern as a function:** `const tr = (key, en, ar) => (locale === "ar" ? ar : t(key, en))` at `page.tsx:87` inlined **both languages at every call site**. 48 keys moved into a new `field-home` namespace registered in both locales; almost all the Arabic already existed inside those `tr()` calls and was **lifted rather than invented** — only the service-unavailable line needed a new translation. Enum labels were **not** given a new namespace: the governed `operational_state` enum is 7 values and **all 7, plus `expired`, the three risk bands and the three visit types, were already translated in `visits.enum`**. The original's `as unknown as Assignment[]` — a banned cast — is gone; `rows.ts` narrows from `unknown` with no cast at all. **Five spec regressions were introduced and all five resolved.** Four were source-scanning contracts asserting on the literal text of `field/page.tsx` (`ai-delta-contract`, `inspector-shell-uplift`, `field-establishment-incidents`, `field-notifications-contract`); the behaviour still holds, so each was **re-pointed at the file that now owns the concern rather than weakened**. **The fifth was a real defect of mine:** `performance-pass4-contract` (K-002) asserts `/field` must **not** declare `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"` because the authenticated layout is meant to infer dynamic rendering — I had copied that line from the expiry route's boilerplate, and removed it. `ai-user-journey` depended on `data-testid="inspector-daily-briefing-panel"` and **the design system has no test-id surface at all, deliberately**; rather than add one, the brief card was given `aria-labelledby` against its own heading and the spec now asserts `getByRole("region", { name: "AI Daily Brief" })` — stronger, because it also proves the a11y contract — with the copy keeping its original casing so the spec's three exact-text assertions still hold. **Two defects found by measuring rather than reading:** the unread indicator was a **bare coloured dot with no text** (WEB-002 §5) and is now a `StatusPill` carrying a label; and the greeting separator was a hardcoded `", "`, so **Arabic rendered a Latin comma** — the whole greeting moved into the message template (`"صباح الخير، {name}"`), which removes the literal and puts the punctuation where a translator owns it. The three off-scale sizes still present anywhere on the route were each traced to their element and **all three are outside this slice** — the shell's mobile-nav drawer (14px), its notification-bell badge (13.33px in **Arial**, the only Arial on the page) and a legacy `t-caption` loader. **Fixed alongside, in the seed:** `01-identity.sql` names every persona in a curated block and a guarded one ending `and full_name like 'Synthetic %'`, but **`inspector1–5` appeared only in the unguarded curated block**, which is precisely why they alone read "Synthetic inspector1" after a provisioner run; the five guarded lines were added with the same names and regions so the blocks cannot disagree. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint PASSED 302 removed (baseline relocked 8114→7812), typography PASSED 206 removed (1542→1336), v5 **75** (was 76), dates 17, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**, temporary axe file removed and confirmed by a **404**, and the browser's theme and locale restored to the state they were found in. **Owed: 200% zoom, browser e2e.** **Parked:** the field header duplicates the AppShell's search and bell (pre-existing, an IA call for the channel migration) · `FieldScopeToggle.tsx` is orphaned · the parallel stylesheet is still linked and comes out last · the shell's own off-scale Arial type. |
| 2026-08-17 | T-137 · the heading sweep under Admin and Inspector | [2026-08-17-T-137-heading-sweep](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-137-heading-sweep.md) | **The owner signed in as Admin and then as Inspector so the routes a Planner cannot reach could finally be tested.** T-136 promoted the shell page title from h2 to h1 and warned that any content heading starting at h3 would become a **skip**; this swept **10 routes across the two roles** with axe against `wcag2a/2aa/21a/21aa/22aa` plus `heading-order`, `page-has-heading-one` and `landmark-no-duplicate-main`. **Two real skips found and fixed, both exactly the predicted shape** — page sections written at h3 under a shell that used to be h2: `/admin/items` went `1→3→3→3→3→3` and `/admin/integrations` `1→3→3→3→2→2`, both now `1→2→2→2→2→2`. **`/admin/items` needed the order to be right:** promoting its five section h3s to h2 would have turned a nested `h4` into a **new** skip, so the h4 was demoted first and the five promoted by id afterwards, with tag balance asserted before and after (`h2 5/5 · h3 1/1 · h4 0`). Everything else was clean: `/admin/planning/expiry`, `/admin/access`, `/admin/packages`, `/admin/localization`, `/admin/workflows`, and `/dashboard` under Inspector — **0 WCAG violations on all ten routes**. **The Inspector pass produced the biggest finding of the accessibility work, and it is not a heading skip: `/field` renders zero headings and zero `role="heading"`.** Every visual heading is a `<div>` or `<span>`, including the page title — `"Good afternoon, …"` is a `<div>` at 16px/700, `"AI Daily Brief"` a `<span>` at 14px/600, `"Today's operations map"` a `<div class="t-label">`, `"Inspections"` a `<div>` at 12.5px — so **a screen-reader user has nothing to navigate the field home by**, which is worse than a skip and **invisible to `heading-order`** because that rule only fires on an increase greater than one. `/field/my-tasks` renders five headings, all h3, with no h1. **The type scale tells the same story in one line**, same session and viewport: `/dashboard` renders `13 · 15 · 20 · 24 · 32` — the approved scale exactly — while `/field/my-tasks` renders `11.5 · 12 · 12.5 · 13 · 13.5 · 14 · 14.5 · 16 · 19`, **nine ad-hoc sizes, five below the 13px floor and weight 700 above the 590 cap**. `/saqeel-ds/saqeel/styles.css` is still linked, so **the T-129→T-132 token promotion — which moved the entire application onto one palette, typeface and scale by retargeting a single file — never reached `/field` at all**, confirming the standing CORRECTION by measurement: that sheet is a separate system rather than a `--sqx-*` override, and only migrating the routes removes it. `/field` is now **the largest single accessibility and design-language gap in the application**, quantified rather than asserted, and giving it a heading structure belongs to its migration (T-023/T-024) rather than to a heading sweep. **A defect in my own work surfaced only because the role changed.** `/admin/planning/expiry` was built in T-127 and ported in T-134 under Planner sessions, which only ever render its **access-refusal** state — so I had never actually seen the screen. The first Admin render showed **"Superseded" truncating to "Supers…" in every row**, and the measurement misled me twice before it settled: the pill root reported `scrollW 89 = clientW 89`, "no overflow", **because the label child absorbed it** (`scrollW 75 > clientW 59`, `overflow:hidden` + `text-overflow:ellipsis`). I checked the root first, disbelieved the screenshot, re-screenshotted, scrolled to prove the renderer was live, and only then inspected the children. Root cause was mine — `colStatus: 12%` leaves a 91px content box after the cell's 24px padding for a pill needing ~101px — fixed by widening Status to 15% and taking 3% from Reason; **0 of 33 status labels truncate now**, and the 4 nodes the detector still flags are the `<caption>` elements deliberately made visually hidden, a false positive since clipped elements always report `scrollWidth > clientWidth`. **A screen you have only seen in one role is a screen you have not seen.** **One tooling finding worth a gate:** `page-has-heading-one` reported nothing across every run in T-135–T-137, including on `/field` which has **zero** headings and `/dashboard` which has no `h1` — it sits outside the WCAG tags and did not run even when requested by rule name, so **any future gate must count `document.querySelectorAll('h1')` directly** rather than trust it. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint 107 removed, typography 129 removed, v5 76 unchanged, dates 17, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**, and the temporary axe file removed with a 404 and clean `git status` after each of the two role passes. **Owed: 200% zoom, browser e2e.** **Parked:** `/field`'s missing heading structure and parallel design system · the 19 `title=""` routes without an `h1` · an h1-counting gate. |
| 2026-08-17 | T-136 · the shell gives 88 routes an `h1` | [2026-08-17-T-136-shell-h1](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-136-shell-h1.md) | **Owner lifted the do-not-touch-the-shell constraint for this one change.** `Shell` rendered the page title as `<Heading level={2} visual="display">`, so **no route using it had an `h1`**. Promoted to `level={1}`: of **107 `<Shell>` call sites, 88 pass a non-empty title and now render an `h1`**, while **19 pass `title=""`** and still have none — those are `title=""` *by design*, because their content owns the page heading, the convention `/execution`, `/analytics` and `AdminDestinationFrame` already use. Verified in the browser: `/planning` h1 1 ("Planning") outline `H1→H2→H3`, `/visits` h1 1 outline `H1→H2→H2`, `/reviews` h1 1 ("Inspection review queue"), each with one `main` and **0 axe violations**; `/operations` and `/factories` measured **h1 0**, confirming they are among the 19 and genuinely have no `h1` anywhere, which now needs a per-route call. **Three lines changed, and the middle one exists because promoting h2→h1 breaks the outline below it.** Any content heading that began at h3 under the old h2 becomes a **skip** under the new h1: `/visits` went from a valid `H2→H3→H2→H2` to an invalid `H1→H3→H2→H2` and axe's `heading-order` rule flagged it. The cause was **`ContextualAiPanel.tsx:39` hardcoding `<h3>`** — the legacy AI panel already sitting in the retirement ledger, superseded by `ai-advisory`/`AdvisoryStrip` which correctly uses `level={2}` — so one line fixed the skip on all **6** of that component's surfaces, and h2 is the right level because it is a page-level section exactly like its replacement. **This is the whole reason the change was tested rather than assumed: a heading skip is not a WCAG AA failure.** axe's WCAG tags were clean before and after, so nothing but the best-practice `heading-order` rule would ever have reported it — which also means **19 files still rendering `level={3}`/`<h3>` under `components/sections`, `visits`, `planning` and `dashboard` may carry latent skips on routes no Planner session can reach**, and that sweep is parked with its one-rule detection method. **A conflict I diagnosed wrongly and reverted in full.** I read `AccessState` as a second source of `h1` and `<main>` on six routes, because six components matching `*AccessState` render inside a `Shell`, and I added a `standalone` prop to switch `<main>`/`h1` to `<div>`/`h2`. Then I checked the import graph: **`components/AccessState` has exactly 2 importers, both the shell-less `/launch` pages** (one mentions "Shell" only inside a comment), and **the six `*AccessState` wrappers render neither `<main>` nor `h1`** — they share a naming pattern with it and nothing else. The conflict never existed, so the prop solved nothing and was unused API on a primitive; reverted in full, with `git diff` on that file empty. **A filename grep is not an import graph** — the instinct to verify before shipping was right and the evidence I started from was wrong. **One real defect fixed on the way:** `/reviews/[id]` computed `ws.title.replace("{factory}", …)` **twice** — once in the route for the shell title and once in `review-workspace` as its `h1` — so the page rendered the same title in two places. Pre-existing and invisible because both were "the page title", and T-136 would have turned it into two `h1`s; fixed by passing `title=""`, which removes the duplication, follows the established convention, and keeps the workspace heading beside its status chips where it belongs. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint 107 removed, typography 129 removed, v5 76 unchanged, dates 17, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**, axe across 4 routes with **0 WCAG violations, 0 heading-order and 0 duplicate-main**, and the temporary axe file removed with a 404 and a clean `git status` confirming no residue. **Owed: 200% zoom, browser e2e.** **Parked:** the 19 `title=""` routes' `h1` story, `/operations` and `/factories` first · the `heading-order` sweep under Admin and Inspector sessions · a script for the axe run. |
| 2026-08-17 | T-135 · `RouteLoading` off the landmark defect, and the axe debt discharged | [2026-08-17-T-135-routeloading-and-axe](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-135-routeloading-and-axe.md) | **Two long-owed items, chosen because they compose.** `components/RouteLoading.tsx` held **four rule breaches in eighteen lines and reached 31 routes**: it rendered its own `<main className="sq-content">` while both shells already render `<main id="main-content">` (`app-shell.tsx:42`, `ShellClient.tsx:649`), so **every importing route served two `main` landmarks while loading**; plus `glyph="◫"` (rule 8 / v5 emoji-as-icon), an `EmptyState` import from the **closed** `components/` root, and the banned `isAr ? ar : en` ternary. Rebuilt on `SkeletonRegion` — the right primitive for a loading state, already rendering `<div role="status" aria-busy aria-live>` with a visually-hidden label — which removed the landmark, the glyph and the closed-directory import at once and made the loading state look like the rest of the system instead of an `EmptyState` with a Unicode square. **No file was added to the closed directory**, verified by `git status`, because `SkeletonRegion` and `Skeleton` bring their own styles so the rebuild needed no CSS module. The `bodyEn`/`bodyAr` props used by two callers were **not dropped** — with bones replacing prose there is nowhere to show that sentence, so it is appended to the announced label and the fact survives even though the pixels changed. **The fourth breach could not be fixed here and the count is the reason:** the 31 callers pass literal `en="…" ar="…"` pairs, roughly **62 hardcoded strings**, so the ternary is the API rather than the file — removing it means changing all 31 callers and giving 31 routes an i18n namespace, which is its own task and is parked with the number rather than half-migrated. **The axe debt is discharged.** `axe-core@4.12.1` was already a dependency; at 572 KB it is too large to inject through one evaluate call, so it was served from `public/` for the run and **deleted immediately, verified three ways** (`rm`, a 404 from the same URL, and a clean `git status`) — a hand-copy that is a trap if repeated, so "put the axe run in a script" is parked. Run against `wcag2a, wcag2aa, wcag21a, wcag21aa, wcag22aa`: **`/dashboard` dark 30 passes / 0 violations, `/dashboard` light 30 / 0, `/dashboard` Arabic RTL 30 / 0, `/factories` 33 / 0** — **zero violations on every surface, in both themes and both directions**, which is the first real axe evidence this programme has and makes the "axe owed" line in eight previous records answerable rather than aspirational. **axe found one genuine defect no gate could see:** `aria-prohibited-attr` on `span[aria-label="Factories shown"]` — `CountBadge` put `aria-label` on a bare `<span>`/`<sup>` with **no role**, which ARIA prohibits, so **the label was being discarded by assistive technology entirely** at both call sites that pass one. This is T-122's ruling arriving from a new direction — *an `aria-*` attribute is a no-op unless the element it lands on can carry it*. Fixed by rendering the number visibly and the label in a `sqx-visually-hidden` span; measured after: visual text "1", announced text "1 Factories shown", `aria-label` absent, `aria-prohibited-attr` gone. **The two `incomplete` results were characterised rather than dismissed:** `aria-valid-attr-value` on `DateRangePicker`'s trigger is `aria-controls` pointing at a React id that does not exist while the popover is closed — axe cannot resolve it and strictly `aria-controls` should reference a present element, so it is a **minor real finding** on a primitive, parked; and `color-contrast` on a `sup` is the superscript `CountBadge` being partially overlapped so axe cannot sample the backdrop, whose contrast was measured by hand in T-130 (7.13 dark / 4.61 light / 10.22 neutral) and passes. **The finding worth more than the fix: `/factories` measures `main: 1, h1: 0`, and axe's own `page-has-heading-one` did NOT flag it** because that rule sits outside the WCAG tags that were run. So the missing-`h1` problem already recorded against `/dashboard` is **not route-specific** — legacy `Shell` renders the page title as `<Heading level={2} visual="display">`, so **all 77 routes importing it have no `h1`**, and `/factories`' outline starts at H2 and never has a level 1. **A clean axe run under the WCAG tags does not mean the heading outline is sound**, which is worth stating plainly because "axe: 0 violations" is exactly the kind of number that stops further looking. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint **107** removed (up from 104 — RouteLoading's comment block went with the rebuild), typography 129 removed, v5 76 unchanged, dates 17, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**. **Owed: 200% zoom, browser e2e.** **Parked:** ~62 hardcoded strings across the RouteLoading callers · **no `h1` on 77 legacy-`Shell` routes**, where one shell-wide fix serves all of them and is now the largest single accessibility win left · `DateRangePicker`'s `aria-controls` · a script for the axe run · `StatusPill` still defaults `ping` to true. |
| 2026-08-17 | T-134 · merging the component ledger's two divergent copies | [2026-08-17-T-134-ledger-merge](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-134-ledger-merge.md) | **`04-COMPONENT-LEDGER.md` had a whole second ledger appended below the first — 574 → 351 lines, 177 unique rows recovered, 0 lost, 0 duplicated.** This is the file whose entire job is *never build what already exists*, and the same component appeared twice with different notes: line 24 carried `Button` with T-040's `name`/`value` work, line 251 carried `Button` with an older, thinner note about `className`, so anyone reading past the halfway point got stale facts about rows the first half described correctly. **Neither copy was a superset, so "take the newer one" would have destroyed 86 rows.** A held 91 rows, B held 173, union 177: **4 existed only in A** (`type`, `route-error`, `dashboard/enforcement-trend`, `dashboard/executive-brief`), **86 only in B** (regulations, enforcement, approvals and factories workspaces), and of the 87 shared rows only **2 differed** — `Button` and `date-range-presets`, A longer in both. **Length is not evidence of supersession**, so for those two A was verified to carry *every* fact B had before B was discarded: `Button` contains B's text verbatim plus the T-040 note, while `date-range-presets` does **not** contain B verbatim, so each distinguishing phrase was probed individually (`Calendar periods are deliberately absent`, `windowDateRangePresets`, `seven, three and zero`, `T-021d` — all present in A, which additionally has `PAST_DATE_RANGE_PRESETS` and T-041). **My first two readings of the file were both wrong and each correction came from measuring rather than assuming:** "a clean 227-line duplication" was a misaligned `diff`, and "copy A is newer, take A" was wrong because B is *longer* (295 section lines vs 227) and holds 86 rows A never had. The heading map settled it — the file is [copy A: title + preamble + sections incl. the newest T-126 rows] + [the preamble AGAIN] + [copy B: sections only] + [a tail unique to neither]. **The merge took B as the base**, substituted A's two superseding rows, inserted `type` before `icon-button` and `route-error` after `trend-bars` (the two tables' row order was compared first and matches exactly, so the insertion points were unambiguous), appended the two dashboard rows where A had them, kept A's title and dropped the duplicated preamble. **Every anchor line was asserted to be the expected row before any write**, so a mismatch exits rather than merging blind; the final assertions were 0 duplicate keys, 0 keys lost against the union, 0 duplicate headings, and 186 rows — the 9 above the union being the tail's T-111/T-122 sections, checked rather than assumed. **The only visible symptom of a 223-line duplication was duplicate headings**, invisible inside a 574-line table, so the ledger now opens with a banner recording the incident and one instruction: search for the row before appending, edit in place if it exists, never paste a section whose heading is already there. **The other four brain files were swept rather than parked** — zero duplicate headings anywhere, so none carries the same shape — **and that sweep corrected a standing figure: the ID collisions are 6, not 3.** `T-026`, `T-027`, `T-046` (**used four times**), `T-077` and `T-078` each name two or more different tasks here, genuine collisions rather than double-logged rows, across 13 rows total. `01-PROJECT-STATUS.md` called the T-076/T-101 incident "the third ID collision"; it is the sixth. That document already prescribed *claim the ID in the tracker at the start of a task* and already observed nothing implements it — T-127…T-134 have each advanced "Highest id in use" by hand, but nothing stops a concurrent session taking the same number, and **a prescription nobody can enforce is not a control**. Cheapest real fix, parked: a gate that fails on a duplicate `T-NNN` in this file, which is the one-line grep that produced the count. **No product code was touched, so no gates apply.** |
| 2026-08-17 | T-133 · retiring 1,394 lines of unreachable design-system CSS | [2026-08-17-T-133-dead-css-retirement](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-133-dead-css-retirement.md) | **`components/saqeel/primitives/primitives.module.css` — 1,301 lines — deleted, plus 93 lines of orphaned tokens and keyframes out of `saqeel.css` (1,023 → 930). 1,394 lines removed, no component and no route touched.** **The full T-077 death checklist was run rather than just the import graph:** 0 code importers (only `use-media-query.ts` is imported from that folder, and it is a `.ts` hook), 0 e2e specs reading it as text, 0 scripts naming it, and **0 rows in either the component or retirement ledger** — the component ledger documents `use-media-query.ts`, which stays. It was never `@retiring`-marked because it was never *replaced*: it was unreachable, and marking it would assert a supersession nobody performed, which is exactly the reasoning T-054 used when it deleted rather than banner-marked. **The independent confirmation came from the gate:** deleting the file moved the typography baseline delta from **115 to 129 violations removed**, because the baseline had recorded that file's 14 `retired-typography-role` entries — **a file that contributes to the baseline is one the scanner really reads**, so the deletion was of something real rather than a phantom. **The orphan scan was deliberately run *after* the deletion**, because beforehand the dead file's own references would have counted as consumers; that ordering is what surfaced **three dead keyframes** (`sqx-flow`, `sqx-sweep`, `sqx-drift` — all defined in `saqeel.css` and referenced nowhere), found by listing every `animation-name` in `src/` and diffing against the `@keyframes` definitions, which chasing tokens alone would have missed. Removed: **13 `--sqx-gradient-*` definitions, 14 declarations** (`--sqx-rim-light`, `--sqx-ease-sweep`, `--sqx-flow-angle/-from/-to`, `--sqx-sweep-start/-end/-skew`) **and the 3 keyframes**. Kept deliberately after checking each: `--sqx-glare-050` and `--sqx-duration-flow` (the skeleton shimmer), `--sqx-duration-sweep` and `--sqx-ease-linear` (the button spinner), and `--sqx-mirror` (Icon, DateRangePicker, SegmentedControl). **Verified structurally rather than by exit code:** braces balanced 26/26, zero malformed declarations, and every surviving token re-read in the browser to confirm it still resolves while every pruned one reads empty. **WEB-002 §2's freeze wording is fixed.** T-131 and T-132 both had to edit `tokens.css` — which the rule called frozen, "never edit it, never add to it, never delete from it" — and both recorded the tension rather than relying on it quietly. §2 now permits **exactly one edit: repointing a declaration at `var(--sqx-*)`**, because that *shrinks* the sheet's authority by moving a decision out of frozen legacy and into the design system, and it is how a change of visual language reached ~80 legacy routes without touching them. A new property, a new raw value or a deletion is still forbidden, and "I was only aliasing" covers none of them. **The freeze exists to stop the legacy sheet accumulating, not deferring.** **A memory-integrity defect was found and deliberately not fixed: `04-COMPONENT-LEDGER.md` contains two divergent copies of itself.** Lines 5–23 repeat at 232–250, then the copies diverge — line 24 and line 251 are both the `Button` row with **different content**, the first citing T-040's `name`/`value` work and the second an older, thinner note about `className`. The file reads as [newer ledger] + [older ledger] appended. **This is the document whose entire job is *never build what already exists***, so a reader consulting the second half gets stale facts about components the first half describes correctly — and it was found precisely *because* the death checklist sends you to the ledger, which turned out to be the unreliable party. Not fixed here on purpose: merging needs a row-by-row decision about which version is authoritative across 574 lines, and a mechanical dedupe would silently destroy the institutional memory the ledger exists to hold. Parked with the exact line ranges. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint 104 removed, typography **129** removed, v5 76 unchanged, dates 17, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**, and `/dashboard` rendered unchanged (7 sections, 112 text nodes, overflow-x false). **Owed: axe, 200% zoom, browser e2e.** |
| 2026-08-17 | T-132 · the frozen type scale defers to the approved one | [2026-08-17-T-132-type-scale-repoint](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-132-type-scale-repoint.md) | **The last piece of the typography divide.** T-131 unified the typeface; the frozen sheet still carried its own sizes, so the routes it styles rendered 14px body and 11.5–12px small text against the approved 15px and 13px floor. Repointed **14 composite shorthands and 34 size/line/weight tokens** at `--sqx-text-*` in one file, with no component touched — the app now has **one typeface, one scale and one palette across migrated and legacy routes alike**. **Measuring consumption first changed the plan.** T-131 had flagged the heading jumps (17→24px, 22→32px) as the danger; they are ~32 call sites. The real mass is **208 consumers below 13px** — `--type-caption-font` alone has **105**, `--type-micro` 76, `--type-label-size` 18 — so the risk was at the small end, not the large one. **Count the consumers before ranking the risk: the scary-looking change was marginal and the boring one was the whole job.** **Three mappings are judgement calls, recorded so they are not "corrected" later.** `caption` and `micro` → `label` (13px) rather than `body` (15px), because their call sites are labels, metadata, ID codes, legend titles and a small button rather than prose — 1px to the accessibility floor instead of 3px; **and this is not the retirement rule**, because WEB-014 §2.1 retires SAQEEL's `--sqx-text-caption` while `--type-caption-font` is a different, frozen-legacy token whose target is a migration decision. `title` (22px) → `heading` (24px) rather than `display` (32px), because the frozen sheet keeps `--type-display` separately and sending both to display would collapse two levels and inflate page titles by 10px. **`--type-input` stays 14px** — that is the reference's control size and the one legacy 14px that is correct. Line-heights for `compact`/`table`/`meta`/`caption` point at `--sqx-text-body-line` (1.6) rather than `label-line` (1.2): the **size** comes from label, the **leading** does not, because those style multi-line content where 1.2 is cramped. **Measured result:** `/factories` renders 13 · 15 · 20 · 24 · 32 with a 13px floor and weights 510/400/590 all within the cap; **document overflow-x false and zero elements past the viewport at 960px, and at 320px the document scrollWidth is exactly 320**. **Arabic keeps the same sizes with looser leading (1.55 / 1.80) and letter-spacing 0** — the thing most likely to have broken, and it held because `tokens.css`'s `[dir="rtl"]` block overrides line-heights only, never sizes. **A 320px overhang was checked before being blamed:** 12 `definition-list` elements overhang by 9px, but that primitive consumes **zero `--type-*` tokens** — only `--sqx-*` — and its 288px width is exactly `--sqx-grid-min-md` (18rem), so the grid's minimum column exceeds the viewport once card inset applies. The document does not scroll, so it is clipped and harmless, but it is a real pre-existing responsive defect and was parked rather than silently fixed. **Two specs pinned the frozen px and were strengthened rather than swapped:** both are named for resolving through canonical tokens, so they now assert the frozen sheet *aliases* `--sqx-text-*` **and** that `saqeel.css` holds the approved values — which fails if an independent scale ever returns, something the literal could never detect. Their comments asserted the *previous* decision ("SAQEEL scale supersedes 32px", "14px body supersedes 16px minimum"); those are now false and were corrected, because **a stale comment on a passing test is worse than a failing one**. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint 104 removed, typography 115 removed, v5 76 unchanged, dates 17, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**, plus measurements at 960px, 320px and in Arabic. **Parked: `DefinitionList` overflows 9px at 320px**, and the note that `tokens.css` is nominally frozen (WEB-002 §2) while T-131 and T-132 have now edited it twice — both edits *reduce* its authority by aliasing, the opposite of what the freeze protects against, but the rule's wording still does not carve that out. **Owed: axe, 200% zoom, browser e2e.** |
| 2026-08-17 | T-131 · one typeface across the legacy routes, and the Arabic defect it exposed | [2026-08-17-T-131-typeface-unification](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-131-typeface-unification.md) | **Opened as "migrate ~80 legacy routes" and measuring first found the job mostly done.** `tokens.css` holds **149 custom properties, 64 of which already alias `--sqx-*`** and only 4 carrying a raw value — so **T-129's palette had already reached every legacy route the day it landed**; colour was never the split. Typography was, and it divided cleanly in two: the **typeface** (`--font-body` was Plex-only, so anything the frozen sheets styled rendered in Plex while migrated routes rendered Inter) and the **sizes** (14px body vs 15px, 28px display vs 32px). **The typeface half was two token lines** — `--font-body` → `var(--sqx-font-sans)` in both the root and the RTL block — and measured on `/factories` it took Latin from **122 Inter / 10 Plex nodes to 132 / 0**, those ten being exactly the `14px` ones still styled by the frozen sheets. **The size half was deliberately not done**: mapping the frozen scale onto the approved one means 17px → 24px headings and 22px → 32px titles across ~80 routes that cannot all be rendered here, which is layout-affecting and wants its own task with per-route measurement rather than a bulk edit — deferred as T-132 with the numbers recorded. **The route-by-route plan would have been eighty tasks for a job that was two lines plus one deferred decision; measure the gap before scoping the sweep.** **Extending the stack to the legacy routes forced a measurement of Arabic, and Arabic was not rendering in Plex.** `next/font` synthesises an **`"<name> Fallback"`** face from a local system font and inserts it immediately after the real one, and that synthetic face **carries Arabic** — so it captured every Arabic glyph before the chain ever reached Plex: measured **49.81px against Plex's 55.33px**, with the DOM stack reading `inter, "inter Fallback", plexArabic, "plexArabic Fallback", …`. **Arabic had been rendering in a system face with the wrong metrics on every migrated route since T-129 — a defect I introduced — while the font stack still named Plex.** Nothing could have caught it by reading: the stack was right, Latin was right, the typography gate checks declarations rather than rendering, and T-129's own Arabic pass measured letter-spacing and digit shape but never the typeface. Fixed with `adjustFontFallback: false` on the Inter loader and verified in both scripts — **Arabic as-declared 55.55 ≈ Plex 55.33 (Inter 48.77), Latin as-declared 101.61 ≡ Inter 101.61 (Plex 103.28)**, with the synthetic face gone from the stack. **WEB-014 §2.0 was corrected**: its T-129 claim that the split "needs no `[lang]` selector" is still true but only because of a setting the rule never named, so the rule now states `adjustFontFallback: false` is load-bearing, carries both measurements, and adds *measure each script separately — Latin passing tells you nothing about Arabic*. **The `design-foundation-contract` font assertion was strengthened rather than swapped**: it now asserts the frozen sheet defers to the design system *and* that `--sqx-font-sans` names both self-hosted faces, so the original test's stated intent — "one self-hosted bilingual metric system" — is better served than before instead of being edited down to whatever shipped. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint 104 removed, typography 115 removed, v5 76 unchanged, dates 17, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**, plus width measurements on `/factories` in English and Arabic. **Parked: T-132 the size repoint**, and the note that `tokens.css` is nominally frozen (WEB-002 §2) while this task edited two lines of it — defensible because aliasing shrinks the sheet's authority rather than growing it, but the rule's wording should carve that out explicitly. **Owed: axe, 320px, 200% zoom, browser e2e.** |
| 2026-08-17 | T-130 · reconciling the shapes the tokens could not carry | [2026-08-17-T-130-shape-reconciliation](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-130-shape-reconciliation.md) | **T-129 changed the language through tokens; this closed the gap the tokens could not reach** — decorative gradients, the rim light, pill geometry and control density, the parts of the grammar that live in component CSS rather than in a variable. **Four files changed, where a much larger sweep was expected, and the reason is the finding: most of the target was already dead.** `components/saqeel/primitives/primitives.module.css` is **1,301 lines with zero importers** — only the `use-media-query` hook is imported from that folder, and it is a `.ts` file, not the stylesheet — so every `--sqx-gradient-cta`/`-sweep`/`-halo`/`-edge`/`-signal` consumer in it and five of its infinite animations are never bundled; `.sqx-btn[data-gradient]` likewise has zero JSX consumers. **The live decorative-gradient surface was exactly one component**, the AI button with one consumer, now flattened to `--sqx-ai-deep` with a hover step, its `::after` glare sweep and the orphaned `button-sweep` keyframe deleted. **The card's elevation was never rendering.** `box-shadow: var(--sqx-shadow-card), var(--sqx-rim-light)` resolved to `inset 0 0 0 1px #23252A, none`, and **`none` is only valid as the sole value of `box-shadow`**, so the entire declaration was invalid and dropped — proved by direct probe rather than inference. **Every Card in the application has been drawing its edge from the `border` alone**, which by accident is exactly the single hairline the new language wants, so nothing looked wrong and nothing would have until someone gave `--sqx-rim-light` a value and every card silently gained a doubled edge. Fixed by making the CSS say what it does (`box-shadow: none`, border stays the hairline, zero visual change); the explain panel, which genuinely floats, took `--sqx-shadow-menu`. **A token that resolves to a keyword is not inert inside a comma list.** **`StatusPill` was rendering at 15px `body`** — prose size on a badge, where the reference specifies 12–13px and the scale floors at 13px — now `label`, measuring 7.13 dark / 4.61 light / 10.22 neutral. **Control density already matched the reference and was left alone**; the one apparent outlier, a 20px button, is `variant="link"` setting `min-block-size: auto` so a text action sits on the baseline of surrounding prose, which WCAG 2.2 §2.5.8 exempts as an inline target. **My own contrast tooling produced a false failure for the second time in two tasks, and that is the durable lesson.** A light sweep reported 11 pill failures at 4.16:1 and one at 1.87:1; every failing row had `backgroundColor: rgba(0,0,0,0)` because the selector matched the **inner label spans** as well as the pill roots, and a transparent background parses to black. Resolved to the nearest **painted** ancestor the real pairs are 4.61 and 10.22, both passing, and the whole page measures **0 failures across 302 elements**. T-129 was the mirror image — the selected segment read 1.3:1 because the lime pill is a **sibling**, so walking *up* missed it. Together: **a contrast check is only as good as its background resolution; never compare against a transparent element's own background, never assume the painted surface is an ancestor, and re-measure a failure before fixing it.** I nearly corrected a palette that was already correct; what stopped it was recomputing the same pair three ways — from hex, from the computed strings, and live — and getting 4.61 each time. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint 104 removed, typography 115 removed, v5 76 unchanged, dates 17, `test:static` **408 passed / 33 failed — exact baseline**, plus rendered checks in both themes with 0 contrast failures and 0 decorative-gradient animations left. **Retirement: `primitives.module.css` is the largest deletion candidate in the design system** and now also carries rules contradicting the language. **Parked: 11 ping dots animate simultaneously on `/dashboard`** — compositor-only and reduced-motion guarded, so not a defect, but the language is deliberately quiet and `StatusPill` defaults `ping` to true; flipping that default touches every pill and is an owner call. **Owed: axe, 320px, 200% zoom, browser e2e.** |
| 2026-08-17 | T-129 · the approved language becomes the system | [2026-08-17-T-129-language-adoption](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-17-T-129-language-adoption.md) | **The manager approved the trial, so the language was promoted into SAQEEL rather than kept beside it.** The headline is architectural: **the entire visual language was replaced by retargeting 128 values in one file.** 254 files consume `var(--sqx-*)` and none holds a literal, so **28 already-migrated routes adopted the new language without one of them being edited** — `/operations` was verified rendering it having never been opened. The no-literals rule had always been justified as tidiness; it is actually what lets the system survive a change of art direction, and WEB-002 §1, CLAUDE.md rule 7 and README rule 7 now say so, with the corollary as the warning: one hardcoded value opts that route out of the *next* change, silently. **SAQEEL is now explicitly the system and `design/linear/` the language**, separated in the rules because the language has been replaced once (IRP → reference) without the system changing at all. **Status was derived and measured, not taken:** the reference calls its green and red *"supporting accents, not status colours"* — guidance for a marketing site that cannot govern a platform where severity is legally meaningful — so success/error/info are its accents verbatim while **`warning` and `major` were derived**, because acid lime **is** the primary action and a warning pill would be indistinguishable from a CTA; all five measure AA on tint in both themes with no two mains closer than ΔE 30. **Two asymmetries were recorded in the rules so they are not "tidied up" later:** `main` is the graphic fill on dark and `dark` the graphic fill on light (warning and info measure 2.25:1 and 2.41:1 on white, under WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 for a dot or bar), and text on a tint uses `darker`/`light`, never `main`. **Brand 600–950 are neutral rather than lime**, because `--sqx-text-link` and `--sqx-text-accent` resolve through them and lime is 1.23:1 on white — no darkened lime stays lime. **`bold` became an alias of `semibold`** since the scale caps at 590 and the token has 254 consumers. **Arabic tracking is zeroed in `:root:dir(rtl)`**, not by a `[dir="rtl"]` override, which WEB-002 §6 forbids. **Inter was self-hosted and proved rendering** by the width comparison that proved it *absent* in T-127 — as-declared 356.27 ≡ Inter 356.27 against Plex 346.16 — with the split needing no `[lang]` selector because Inter ships no Arabic and falls through per glyph. **Four defects found by measuring and none by a gate.** White ink on the new lime fill measured **1.1:1**, because repointing brand to a *light* colour broke every token assuming "brand fill is dark, so ink is white" — light theme went 2 failures → 0 across 90 elements. **Chromatic link text survived in the dark block only** (`--sqx-text-link`, `-accent`, `--sqx-action-tertiary-text`, `--sqx-segment-label` → pale lime), so "How is this calculated?" rendered lime against the rule written in the same session; **the light block was already correct, which is exactly why it survived — every check that looked at light passed.** And two specs pinned values that had to move: `design-foundation-contract` pins the grey hexes to guard INSP-702 (disabled text ≥4.5:1) and was re-pointed **only after proving the requirement still holds** under the new ramp (5.77 light; 4.72/5.52/6.13 dark) rather than updating the expectation to whatever shipped, while `ipad-pwa-shell-contract` pins the PWA dark theme colour and was found **only because the tally read 407 where baseline was 408** — "the suite looks green" would have missed it. **One measurement was wrong and is recorded rather than buried:** the contrast scan reported the selected segment at 1.3:1 in dark, a false positive, because the lime pill is a **sibling `<span>`** that slides behind the segment, so walking the ancestor chain for a non-transparent background finds the graphite root and misses the pill; Replaced with an exact cell match, verified in the DOM to resolve to one row. **Three `.sq-banner--success` assertions were re-pointed to `section(page).getByRole("status")` before the code was called done**, not after a failure; the fourth occurrence belongs to `/admin/planning/lookups` and was left alone. Both spec edits were made **by line number through a script because the file is CRLF** — the trap this repo has hit four times — and each asserted its own landing. **`test:static`'s 408 proves nothing about `cd-044`, which is not in the hand-maintained allowlist** (the exact T-124 trap), so the contract was verified **live in the browser** instead: section with the target heading, the v1 row, `Enable`/`Disable`/`New version`, the form inside the section, `input[name="reason"]`, a submit labelled exactly `Create version (disabled)`, and all seven control names matching what `actions.ts` parses — each control with a wired `<label for>`. **Arabic measured: `nonZeroLetterSpacing: 0`** (the WEB-011 no-tracking rule holds via an RTL token reset), **92 Arabic-Indic digit nodes**, and the only 30 Latin-digit nodes are seeded `reason` values, which are database content rather than copy. **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint 104 removed, typography 115 removed, v5 **76 unchanged** with none in a touched file, `verify:dates` 17 passed, `test:static` 408 passed / 4 skipped unchanged, rendered and measured in dark, light, English and Arabic. **The typography gate now exempts `components/experimental/linear/`** as the parallel of `saqeel/type/` — a *detection* reduction, which the WEB-014 §8 ratchet does not govern and T-102 warned about, justified only while the directory is a sanctioned trial and **to be deleted with it if the experiment is rejected**. **Owed: axe, 320px and 200% zoom, browser e2e, and first-load numbers.** **Open for the manager: the density question** (`design.md` is a marketing-site reference — 96px band gaps, 64–72px hero type, *"information density stays low"*, *"never uses 3-column card grids"* — applied here to four dense governed tables; the band rhythm was used at page-region level only, and a literal reading needs a different information architecture, not different CSS), **whether to self-host Inter**, and **that the reference system has no Arabic story at all** — no Arabic typeface and a scale whose negative tracking must be zeroed — which for an Arabic-first ministry platform means authoring its Arabic half from scratch. |

WEB-008's first standing sweep — diff what the page loads against what it renders — is a two-line grep and would have caught it. **The fix is two surfaces on query state.** `/admin/packages` is a register of one row per package; the workbench opens on `?package=&version=&tab=` with Designer / Field preview / Publish impact / Versions. **Routes are fixed and CLAUDE.md names query state as the sanctioned mechanism**, so nothing was nested; `?package=` is the key rather than the critique's `?version=` because a package with no versions has no version id and is the case that needs the workbench most. **The Designer tab does not exist for a version that cannot be edited** — a published version gets three tabs and an inline notice saying why, not a disabled fourth. **Register: 6,810 → 1,977px, inputs 103 → 32, visible required 20 → 0, open destructive forms 9 → 0, inline designers 2 → 0, buttons 61 → 6, leaf nodes 332 → 129, heading skips 2 → 0.** **`PackagePreview` keeps the field-workspace look** — the owner's ruling, settling T-125's parked question. It gained `headingLevel` so one component sits at H3 as its own tab and H5 inside the designer's third pane, which is what removed both heading skips. **T-125's other parked item is cleared**: `DraftEditor`'s 22 `btn`, 5 `sq-field`, 4 `sq-input`, `sq-select`, `sq-choice` and `sq-panel` are now `Field`/`TextInput`/`SaqeelSelect`/`Choice`/`Button`/`IconButton`, taking the route's legacy classes 40 → 5 — the 5 being `PackagePreview`'s, which the ruling protects. That pushed the file to 429 lines, past the 400 ceiling, so the item-rule panel and action-forms pane moved to `_designer/` — an underscore folder, so App Router adds no segment. **Two measurement corrections worth keeping.** `getBoundingClientRect()` reported **12 visible required fields** on the register; `checkVisibility()` reports **0** — content in a closed `<details>` sits under `content-visibility: hidden`, keeping a layout box and a non-null `offsetParent` while being unrendered and unfocusable. **A rect is not a visibility test.** And my first spec sweep found **four** broken assertions where there were **five**: it extracted string literals with a regex, which mis-parsed `"…!== \"draft\""`. Handing each literal to `eval` found the fifth. **Regex is the wrong tool for reading JS string literals.** **Verified:** typecheck 0, lint 97 removed, typography 115 removed (route measures 0), v5 **76 unchanged**, static suite **408 passed / 33 failed unchanged — which proves nothing about `cd-008-009-packages`, still not in the allowlist**; the literal-evaluating sweep (36 in-scope assertions, 0 broken) is what covers it. Only its 12 typography violations were replaced. **The `let` ban was satisfied by structure, not suppression** — `addSection`'s `let key` + `let suffix` unique-key loop became a `Set` of taken keys plus a small recursive `nextFreeKey`. **Two process notes, both recorded rather than buried.** I hit the **zero-match-reports-success** shape again: an import was anchored to `import styles`, which `PackagePreview` does not contain, so the replace was a no-op and the script logged *"import added"* — caught only by the next typecheck resolving `Text` to the DOM `Text` interface. **This document records that shape four times already and this is the second occurrence in two sessions**, so every replace helper now asserts its result.
