T-113 and T-115's test — count the non-zero categories before choosing a form — rules out all six. **The screen had four names**: rail *Survey Configuration*, page *Inspection Forms*, loading *Package library & designer*, body *Inspection packages, tracked by version*. Now one. **Effective-date windows were being evaluated in UTC** — `currentPublished` used `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)`, so between 21:00 and midnight Riyadh the "currently published" version could resolve to the wrong one; now `riyadhToday()` from `lib/dates.ts`, threaded through as `today`. **Three tabs left the route** plus a second three-step "configuration journey" strip that also left — the third occurrence of that defect; the tab row is now the four package states, as URL state. **The heading outline ran `H1 → H2 → H3 → H4 ×11 → H3 → H2 → H2`**, with *Publish impact* as eleven identical H4s; now one H2 per package card. Route file **574 → 40**, typography **+11 removed** (38 remain, all in T-125 files), eslint **+7**, i18n **0 → 86 keys × 2**. **Sweep 2 found 9 pinned specs and this task wrongly reported that none needed re-pointing** — **corrected by T-125**, which found six broken assertions in `cd-008-009-packages.spec.ts` (`WRITER_ROLES` as a `new Set([…])` literal, `version.status === "draft" && canWrite`, `canWrite && <section`, `mandatoryWhenVisible`, `scoringEnabled: response.scoring_enabled !== false`, and `aria-busy="true"` in `loading.tsx`). **The verification was the error, not the sweep:** that spec is not in the static allowlist, so "the static suite is unchanged at 408" could never have covered it, and its own tests cannot run here because the describe block needs a browser. Re-pointed in T-125; `aria-busy` was not a behavioural regression because `SkeletonRegion` sets it. **`Definition` is mirrored in `view.ts` rather than imported from `DraftEditor`**, whose types are module-private — a feature module importing from an app component would invert the layering (WEB-000 §6); the duplication is deliberate and collapses in T-125. **Two mistakes, both caught by measuring and neither by a gate.** Deleting `packages.module.css` returned a **500**, because `page.tsx` no longer imported it but `DraftEditor.tsx:6` still does — T-093's lesson exactly (*acting on the tool's answer would have deleted three live stylesheets*), and one grep would have caught it; restored with `git show`, and its 2 typography violations therefore belong to T-125. And **making the states mutually exclusive silently changed what "Draft" counts**: a package holding both a publish and an open draft counted only as published, so the filter read **0** where the old metric read **2**. **A filter is not a partition** — `draft` now means *has an open draft*, the question someone actually asks. Caught by comparing the rendered count against the old screen, not by any check. **Verified live:** status 200, impact panels 0 of 11 open, Reconstruction note 0, *Boundaries kept visible* 0, filters `all` 11 cards / `filter=draft` 3 / `q=UAT` 3; Arabic `lang=ar dir=rtl` with title, filters and governance all Arabic, Arabic-Indic digits in the cards and **zero untranslated English** (the Latin that remains is package codes and version labels, correct per WEB-013 §3); RTL filter order mirrors 1108 → 1023 → 940 → 834; **overflow-x 0 and zero elements outside the viewport at both 320px and 1440px, in LTR and RTL**. **Owed: axe on all four states, a native Arabic review of the 86 keys I wrote, browser e2e (still the repo-wide chromium blocker), and the first-load number.** **Parked: T-125** — `DraftEditor` (18 KB), `PackagePreview`, `PublishControls`, `TemplateRegistry`, `ImpactPanel` and `packages.module.css` carry **38 typography violations and ~60 eslint findings**; `PackagesEditors.tsx` and `features/admin-packages/editor-strings.ts` exist only to bridge to them and both disappear with that task. **Also parked: the rail still says *Survey Configuration***, and unlike T-123's entry it carries a `businessTab` grouping key, so renaming the label alone would leave the two inconsistent — owner call. |
| 2026-08-16 | T-123 · `/admin/localization`, and a screen that shipped 1,821 rows to draw 12 | [2026-08-16-T-123-localization-migration](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-16-T-123-localization-migration.md) | **The route serialised its entire dictionary so the browser could slice it.** Search, filter and pagination were `useState`, so all **1,821 `ui_strings` crossed to the client to render 12 rows** — a **755 KB document**, which is WEB-005 indefensible and WEB-004 backwards, since the ladder already puts URL state above `useState`. Moved to **URL state with server-side slicing**: document **755 KB → 336 KB**, rows in payload **1,845 → 41**, and the route became deep-linkable. **Search is a plain GET form**, so it works with JavaScript off; the trade — filtering is now a navigation rather than an instant local operation — was taken deliberately and written into the record. **CSV export could no longer be built in the browser** because the client no longer holds the rows, so it became a **route handler** that re-reads the full dictionary and applies the same filter — which also deleted a `document.createElement("a").click()` (WEB-012) and made the export honour the current view instead of whatever sat in client memory. **On charts, two earned their place and four did not.** Built: **Arabic coverage 1,797/1,821 = 99%** and **Reviewed 0/1,797 = 0%**, both single governed ratios with real denominators, which is exactly what `Gauge` exists for — and together they say the thing the screen was hiding, that everything is translated and **nothing has been reviewed**, previously available only by noticing `REVIEWED 0` and dividing. Declined with reasons: a status distribution (5 states, **2 non-zero, one at 98.7%** — one full bar, a sliver and three nothings), a donut (same skew, and `Donut` caps at 3 categories against 5 states), a time series (`updated_at` is *last touched*, not an event log — the real per-key history RPC sits behind the history panel and would be a new query), and a length histogram (bucketing past the **1.3 ratio that already exists in code** would be inventing bands). **That 1.3 layout-risk ratio was computed per row at edit time and never aggregated**, so nobody could know how many keys were at risk without opening all 1,821 — now one reduce over rows already in memory, no new query and no invented threshold. **Two tabs pointed at the same URL** (*Reference lists* and *Language & translations* were both `/admin/localization`, so one did nothing) and a third left the route — T-122's defect found again; the tab row is now the five translation states, the one thing that genuinely changes the panel below it. **The page was titled "Reference Lists"**, subtitled *"Shared reference data used across the platform"*, above the translation registry. **Both WEB-008 sweeps paid out on their first real use:** the data-vs-render diff found the discarded state counts and the risk ratio; the `e2e/` source-path grep found **6 pinned specs** including a dedicated `web-admin-m9-localization.spec.ts`, and **four were re-pointed before the code changed, so none ever went red in a run** — the opposite of T-122, where one surfaced as three red tests afterwards. **Primitives were wrapped, not extended:** `TextInput` and `Text` take no `dir`/`lang` and both APIs are deliberately closed, so Arabic fields sit inside a `<span dir="rtl" lang="ar">` — both attributes inherit — rather than widening a shared primitive unasked (T-087's reversibility tie-break). **The design-registry markers were restored after I dropped them:** `data-saqeel-design="WA-DES-010"` and its hash identify the approved *design*, not the implementation, so they carry across a rebuild and the spec asserting them is right to. Route file **248 → 32**, largest component **531 → 122**, typography **31 → 0**, eslint **+33 removed**, i18n **0 → 92 keys × 2** with `copy(en, ar)` ~45 → 0 and `t(key,"English")` ~90 → 0, **`useEffect` 4 → 0** (the `setAr(row.ar)` prop-sync is gone because the list keys by `row.key`, so React remounts and state resets by construction) and the one `eslint-disable-next-line` removed with the effect it was silencing. **Process failure worth recording: I hit the CRLF zero-match trap** while re-pointing the specs — `\n`-joined patterns matched nothing against CRLF files and the script **reported success on four untouched files**. This document records that exact shape three times (T-058, T-076, T-090) and I walked into it anyway; it was caught only because the re-run still failed. The re-point script now normalises line endings before matching. **Owed: axe on all four states, a native Arabic review of the 92 keys I wrote, browser e2e (5 tests still blocked repo-wide on chromium), and the first-load number.** **Parked: the shell rail still calls this route *Lookup Management*** while the page is now *Language & translations* — an owner call, out of scope under "do not touch the shell". |
| 2026-08-16 | T-122 · `/admin/access` onto SAQEEL, and six ways of saying the same thing become one | [2026-08-16-T-122-admin-access-migration](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-16-T-122-admin-access-migration.md) | Owner-approved four-state migration — permission required, error boundary, skeleton, loaded screen. **Route file 391 → 25 lines** against a 40-line ceiling; largest component 265 → 184; **typography debt 13 → 0**; **i18n 0 → 109 keys × 2 locales** with `t(key,"English")` **120 → 0** and the local `copy(en, ar)` helper gone; native `<select>` **4 → 0**, `document.getElementById` **3 → 0**, inline style literals **30 → 0**; new tree measures **11 files, 0 ESLint findings**. **The route-owned frame turned out to be two thirds already built:** `shell-page-frame` has sat at **zero importers** since T-004 — which `05-RETIREMENT-LEDGER.md` records as its *expected* state — and already implements the gutter clamp, breadcrumb, title and description, so `access-frame` composes it and adds only the metric band and tabs. **That is the swap the ledger has prescribed all along ("each future screen migration swaps one `Shell` for one `ShellPageFrame`") and this is the first one taken.** **`AdminDestinationFrame` was marked, not edited** — it has **5 consumers**, and editing it would have silently restyled `/admin/integrations`, `/admin/localization`, `/admin/packages` and `/admin/risk`, so it carries an `@retiring` banner naming those four `pending`; the shared `AdminRouteBoundary` was likewise left alone and a route-owned `AccessDenied` built, which **is reachable** because the parent layout admits `admin` *and* `supervisor` while this route admits `admin` only. **Six restatements of three facts collapsed to one card**, and one of the deletions was not a duplicate at all: the **"Reconstruction note" was internal build commentary rendered to ministry users**, and the **gate banner was unconditional**, so read-only viewers were told every write is guarded when they cannot write at all — the governance card is now role-aware (`rls` + `readOnly` for a reader, `rls` + `audit` + `effect` for a manager). **A role is not a status:** the admin badge was `badge-warning`, a warning token spent on identity, which is T-116's defect a second time; role keys now render `StatusPill` accent/neutral with the access class in `title` and the key itself in `Mono` because it is a machine identifier. **Nothing formats inline** — every date through `formatDate(value, locale)` and every count through `formatCount(value, locale)`, where the old code called **`new Date(...).toLocaleDateString()` with no locale argument at all**, rendering Latin digits and a Gregorian calendar on the Arabic screen; three table headers (`"Capability"`, `"Source"`, `"Actions"`) were hardcoded English inside the component and rendered English under `dir="rtl"`. **The heading outline was fixed by being forced to: `Text as="h3"` does not typecheck**, because `TextElement` excludes headings — the primitive refusing to let a heading be faked — so the `h1 → h3` skip became `h1 → CardHeader level="h2" → Heading level={3}` by construction. **Measured on the server payload and a computed-style probe:** reconstruction note 0, old RLS alert 0, gate banner 0, legacy classes (`t-caption`/`sq-lozenge`/`sq-table`/`btn-primary`/`<select>`) all 0, inline px/hex literals 0, and **page gutter `padding-inline` 32px/32px where it was 0px/0px** — the defect visible in the owner's screenshot. **Two source-text specs had to be re-pointed and one was missed in the first sweep**, surfacing as 3 new static failures before being caught: `admin-access-route-aware` (re-pointed and grown 3 → 4 tests, the new one asserting `en`/`ar` key-set equality and that every Arabic error string actually contains Arabic) and `execution-access-contract`. **This is precisely the 146-spec tax T-119 predicted, charged on the very first migration after it, and it is the strongest argument yet for auditing a route's specs as part of its inventory.** Static suite **407 → 408 passed**, 33 failed all pre-existing; v5 unchanged at 77 with none in a touched file; `actions.ts` and `role-capability-actions.ts` deliberately untouched because the guarded RPCs are the write authority. **Blocked and stated rather than hidden: the rendered check could not be completed** — the Browser pane's `visibilityState` is `hidden`, which stalls the client transition and holds the **parent** `/admin` fallback open, the exact caveat recorded against T-096, and that fallback is also where `mains: 2` came from (`RouteLoading`'s second `<main>`, a ~25-route defect this document already carries). **Owed before done: axe on all four states in both themes and directions, a native Arabic review of the 109 keys I wrote, a 320px pass in a visible viewport, e2e (still blocked repo-wide on browsers and credentials), and the first-load number as a measurement request.** |
| 2026-08-16 | T-121 · `npm run lint` exists, runs, and is a ratchet rather than a wall | [2026-08-16-T-121-eslint-ratchet](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-16-T-121-eslint-ratchet.md) | ESLint 9 flat config on 7 devDependencies, replacing T-118's failing stub, with the WEB-000 rules expressed as `no-restricted-syntax` entries **whose messages cite the law they enforce** (`WEB-000 §4: no let in .tsx`, `WEB-000 §3: no as unknown as`, `WEB-002 §5: no <svg>`, `WEB-001 §2: no "use client" in a route file`). **The design problem was not the rules, it was the 8,114 errors a fresh run reports** — and shipping a second permanently-red gate would have repeated the exact defect recorded against `npm run gates`, which has sat at 77 unclearable findings for this whole programme. So `check-eslint.mjs` **mirrors `check-typography.mjs` exactly**: same `file::rule` count keys, same `--update` flag, same failure output, same *"violation(s) removed since the baseline"* line, so an agent that understands one understands the other and **WEB-014 §8 carries over verbatim — the baseline may only go down.** **Zero-comments has no ESLint equivalent** (core rules cannot see comments as findings) and became a **custom inline flat-config plugin, adding no dependency**, honouring both sanctioned exceptions — the regex-locked `@retiring` banner and TSDoc blocks **only** under `components/saqeel/` — and reporting `eslint-disable`/`@ts-ignore` separately as WEB-000 §3 suppressions. **325 of the first run's 8,439 errors were artifacts, and both diagnoses are findings about the codebase rather than concessions:** 189 + 12 came from `<Text role="bodyStrong">`, because **SAQEEL's typography `role` prop collides with the ARIA global attribute** (fixed properly with `ignoreNonDOM: true`, which keeps every real ARIA check), and 124 came from `useT()`, **an async *server* helper named like a React hook** — demoted to `warn` rather than disabled, because the rule is right that the name reads as a hook and **the name is the defect**, a rename across 81+ call sites that is its own task. **Severities were chosen by measuring per area first, and migrated code turned out nearly clean:** `max-lines` is **already 0** across the design system, `features/*`, `i18n`, `lib` and every migrated section, so the file-budget rule costs nothing to enforce today and all 97 findings are in unmigrated code; **`jsx-a11y/alt-text` is already 0 across all 814 `.tsx` files**, so WEB-003's alt-text law is a guard here, not a backlog. Remaining debt is **90% comments** — 7,334 of 8,114, densest in `/field` (2,270) and `i18n + lib` (1,847) — which the ratchet retires file by file as the programme migrates. **The ratchet was proved by injecting a defect rather than by reading an exit code (T-107):** a probe component carrying a `let`, an `as unknown as` and a needless mutable binding was written into `components/saqeel/`, the gate **failed with exit 1 naming all three with their WEB-000 citations**, the probe was deleted and the gate returned to PASSED — with the injection confirmed to have landed, which is the T-090 shape that has burned this repo four times. `verify` now includes `lint`, which it could not before. **Process finding worth more than the config: the install first landed in the wrong `package.json`.** The repository root and `apps/web` are **separate npm projects with no `workspaces` field**, and a shell whose working directory had reset installed all seven packages at the root; caught when `eslint --format=json` could not find the config, undone with `npm uninstall` at the root, and the root manifest and lockfile verified unmodified against git afterwards. **Use `npm --prefix apps/web` for every npm command here — there are two manifests and nothing links them.** **Parked:** rename `useT`; **16 `eslint-disable` directives in `src/` are already dead**, suppressing rules that report nothing; `e2e/m9-challenger.js:317` does not parse (a `.js` file holding TypeScript); and type-aware linting was deliberately deferred until the baseline is falling rather than growing. **Blocked and unchanged: `npm run gates` is still red on the same 77 `check:design-system-v5` findings, and because `verify` runs `gates` first, `lint` is never reached by `verify` even though it passes standalone.** Those 77 need clearing or a baseline of their own — the last thing between this programme and a Definition of Done anyone can satisfy. |
| 2026-08-16 | T-120 · `/field`'s parallel design system does not override SAQEEL | [2026-08-16-T-120-field-design-system-ruling](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-16-T-120-field-design-system-ruling.md) | **The blocker this programme recorded on 2026-08-12 does not exist, and 658 violations — 43% of the whole baseline — have been held back on a false premise.** `01-PROJECT-STATUS.md` states that migrating `/field` is "work against a live override" because `public/saqeel-ds` "redefines `--font-*` and `--type-*` for the whole subtree", so "the primitives would render inside a scope that has already replaced the tokens they consume." **The tokens the primitives consume are `--sqx-*`, and the parallel system does not define one of them.** Measured both directions: saqeel-ds defines **113** tokens, `saqeel.css` defines **479**, and the intersection is **0**; the string `sqx` does not occur anywhere in `public/saqeel-ds`. What it *does* override is **109 tokens of the frozen `tokens.css`** — the sheet this programme is deleting anyway. `saqeel.css` is imported at `src/app/layout.tsx:2`, above every route including `/field`, so a `<Text>` there resolves its role exactly as it does on `/dashboard`. **The parallel sheet styles element selectors** — `:root`, `body`, `h1`–`h6`, `p`, `a`, `::selection` — and a primitive's CSS Module class (0,1,0) beats every one of them (0,0,1) regardless of load order, so it governs bare elements and legacy `t-*` classes and stops governing each element the moment that element becomes a primitive. **That makes the migration incremental and self-limiting**: each migrated file is a file the parallel sheet no longer reaches, there is no cutover, and the `<link>` comes out last rather than first. The ruling — join SAQEEL or be declared separate — is still the owner's, but the second option is no longer the pragmatic one by default. **Owed, and stated rather than hidden: this is a specificity argument, not a measurement**, and this document's own rule is that *a font claim is a width measurement or it is a guess* (T-095). The measurement is one computed-style read of a primitive on a `/field` route against the same primitive on `/dashboard`; it could not be taken because `/field` needs an authenticated Inspector session and the persona credentials are absent (T-119). **Parked: `/field` holds the two largest files in the repository** — `Workspace.tsx` (1,991 lines / 130 KB) and `Startup.tsx` (1,384 / 85 KB) — and is the offline PWA channel, so whatever the ruling those are the highest regression risk here and must not share a task with anything else; and `public/saqeel-ds/saqeel/tokens/fonts.css` **still `@import`s three families from `fonts.googleapis.com`**, so the offline channel still carries the runtime network dependency the root layout removed by self-hosting. |
| 2026-08-16 | T-119 · the specs were pinned to markup seven tasks had already deleted | [2026-08-16-T-119-stale-source-contract-specs](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-16-T-119-stale-source-contract-specs.md) | **"E2E owed" on T-111 … T-117 was three debts wearing one name, and only one of them was an agent's to close.** Browsers were **never installed** (`Executable doesn't exist at …ms-playwright\chromium-1228`); **persona credentials are absent** — `personas.ts` resolves `password` from the environment and throws when unset, neither `SAQEEL_TEST_PASSWORD` nor any `SAQEEL_TEST_<ROLE>_EMAIL` is in `.env`, there is no `.env.local`, and every spec in the `e2e` project depends on `auth.setup.ts`, so **the entire authenticated suite is unrunnable**; and the specs themselves assert markup the rebuilds deleted. **A useful correction: the suite does *not* need a production build.** `playwright.config.ts` runs `node .next/standalone/server.js` and no standalone build exists, but `reuseExistingServer` defaults on and reuses a dev server on 127.0.0.1:3000 — so WEB-006 §3 does **not** make e2e agent-forbidden; credentials do. `playwright.static.config.ts` runs 444 source-contract tests with no browser and no auth, and measured **35 failed → 33**, **405 passed → 407**. `analytics-journey-contract.spec.ts` asserted `className="kpi-grid"`, `className="panel kpi"`, `"Export · unavailable"` and `<code className="id-code">{metric.trace}</code>` — **every one deleted by T-111** — and rather than delete the spec or transcribe the new markup, each ANL-S01 claim was traced to its new home (states → `resolveAnalytics`/`analytics-screen`/`loading`/`error`; lineage → `analytics-blocked.tsx:101`; definitions → rates and counts; export-unavailable and no-live-AI → `bottlenecks.ts`), **2 tests → 5**. **Two assertions are now stronger than what they replaced:** the bottleneck contract is checked by **importing `ANALYTICS_BOTTLENECKS` and asserting the data**, which cannot drift when markup changes, and a new test asserts **`en`/`ar` key-set equality** for `analytics.json`, which nothing checked before. **`platform-design-system-contract.spec.ts` was failing on Windows only, for a reason unrelated to what it tests** — `path.relative` returns backslashes, so every comparison against a `"src/app/…"` literal missed; the guard **passed on Linux CI and failed on a workstation**, in two separate tests, and is fixed with a `relativePosix` helper. That repair exposed a real finding the design-system gate cannot see: **`src/app/global-error.tsx` carries 7 literal hex colours**. It is **admitted to the allowlist rather than stripped**, because it renders its own `<html>`/`<body>` for the case where the root layout itself threw, so no stylesheet is guaranteed loaded and no `var(--sqx-*)` is guaranteed to resolve — the same argument that already admits `layout.tsx`, and the only other such file in `src/**.tsx`. **The structural finding is the one that matters for what comes next: 146 of 252 specs assert the spelling of source files.** Every migration breaks the ones pinned to its markup, nothing re-points them, and the resulting red is indistinguishable from a real regression — a tax on the migration about to be scaled up, and precisely the class of assertion T-063 and T-078 already retired twice. **The remaining 33 static failures are older drift, not this cycle's**: sampled four — `design-foundation-contract:112` expects `sb.from("factories").select("region")` in a shell query that now uses `cache`, `responsive-dashboard-operations:9` expects `BUSINESS_ROLE_KEYS.includes(role)`, `shell-f0-design-system:56` and `compliance-shared-shell:44` expect `className="panel"` — all predating the charting tasks, so not chased (WEB-006 §1). |
| 2026-08-16 | T-118 · the five commands the rules require and nobody could run | [2026-08-16-T-118-missing-npm-scripts](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-16-T-118-missing-npm-scripts.md) | `npm run verify`, `lint`, `test`, `unit` and `budgets` now exist; scripts **15 → 23**. **`lint` is a failing stub, deliberately, and the reasoning is the reusable part.** ESLint is not unwired, it is **absent** — no `eslint` dependency, no config file, no binary — and `next lint` was removed in Next 15, so **wiring a script cannot create a toolchain**. Three options were weighed: install four devDependencies (satisfies the rule, but adding them to a government platform repo is an owner call), omit `lint` and amend WEB-006 to match (**edits the law to fit the code**, a larger decision than installing a linter), or a stub that fails loudly and prints exactly what is missing — **taken**, because it converts an invisible gap into a visible one, which is the whole point of T-107's lesson that *a rule naming a command nobody can run is not a control*. **`budgets` fails for a structural reason, not a tooling gap:** WEB-005 §1's per-route first-load JS and CSS are read from a production build, and `npm run build` is human-only under WEB-006 §3 — so `budgets` **cannot be an agent command in any form**, and the script now says so instead of not existing. **`verify` excludes both on purpose** — a permanently red `verify` is an ignored `verify` — and is `gates → verify:dates → test`, all of which genuinely run. **Three working scripts and two Playwright configs had no npm entry point at all** and are now reachable: `verify-dates.mjs` (**17 assertions, passing**), `verify-touch-targets.mjs`, `playwright.static.config.ts` → `test:static`, `playwright.unit.config.ts` → `test:unit` (**13 passed**). ⚠ **Security finding raised, not acted on: two committed files carry plaintext seeded credentials** — `scripts/verify-admin.mjs:8` (1 password) and `scripts/audit-v5-a11y.mjs:14-19` (**all five personas**) — which is exactly what `e2e/personas.ts` was rewritten to remove, and its own header explains why: **the values are already in git history, so this needs rotation, not deletion.** Neither file was wired into an npm script for that reason. **Parked: WEB-006 §3 tabulates seventeen gates under `scripts/gates/` and that directory does not exist** — two real gate scripts exist and live in `scripts/`, so the rule table describes an intended architecture as though it were current. **Blocked, and unchanged by this task: `npm run gates` exits 1 for everyone** on 77 pre-existing `check:design-system-v5` findings naming no file any recent task touched, so **no task can satisfy WEB-006 §5** and every session reporting "gates green" is reading past an exit code. |
| 2026-08-16 | T-117 · The bar charts read left-to-right in Arabic | [2026-08-16-T-117-bar-series-rtl](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-16-T-117-bar-series-rtl.md) | T-112 fixed the label *collision* in RTL and left the plot itself running left-to-right — correct geometry, backwards reading order. **There is no CSS route:** Recharts has no writing-mode support, so `dir` on an ancestor does nothing to a plot; four props have to flip together — `YAxis orientation`, `XAxis reversed`, the chart margin, and the tick `textAnchor`. **`LabelList position` is useless under `reversed`** — `"left"` and `"right"` both resolve to the bar's *base*, measured dropping the value at 398–429 against labels starting at 401, a straight collision — so it was replaced with a custom `ValueLabel`. **Two wrong guesses before measuring settled the geometry:** `x - GAP` put the value inside the bar, `x - width - GAP` overshot past the labels; under `reversed` Recharts keeps `x` at the **base** and makes **`width` negative**, so `x + width` is the growing tip in *both* directions and the renderer needs no branch for position at all — only the gap sign and the anchor flip. **`isRtl` could not live in `lib/i18n`**: that module imports `next/headers`, so a client chart importing it fails the build outright — the dev server went to a 500 until it moved to `i18n/direction.ts`, a pure function over a type-only import. **`direction: ltr` on `.tick` stays and is not redundant** — SVG resolves `text-anchor` against the inline base direction, so without it `"start"` extends the label leftwards into the bars, re-creating the exact T-112 defect. Verified by measurement in both directions: **ar** labels start 401, bars grow 393 → 292, every value clear of both its tip and the labels across 3 charts on `/dashboard`; **en** unchanged at labels ending 154, bars 161 → 262, values from 268. **axe 0 violations in RTL on both routes**; v5 unchanged at 77. **Same task, second report: was the `/analytics` "Source degraded" block junk?** Checked before touching it, because two of those words are separable. **The banner is not junk and stays** — it is gated on `source_status === "unavailable"` from the RPC, data-driven rather than the T-110 always-on-banner shape, and verified live appearing when degraded and vanishing when the source recovered; deleting it would hide a real degradation. **`reviews_role_scope` is junk** — `affectedSource` was `unavailable_reason ?? metric_key`, a raw database object name piped straight into a sentence in front of a ministry user, unlocalised by construction and mildly disclosing schema (WEB-008 §2: never render a raw database value as a label). **And "Source degraded" rendered twice** — once as the header `StatusPill` and again as the banner's own title, verbatim. The banner now carries the part the pill cannot: *what it means for the figures* ("Every figure below is still governed. Metrics with no source are listed as blocked, never as zero."), the failing source is `console.warn`ed for an operator so the diagnostic survives without being copy, and `affectedSource` is gone from the loader's return type and both view layers. Measured in Arabic with the source genuinely degraded: **pill occurrences 1, new title and body present, raw identifiers 0.** **Parked: `Donut`, `Gauge` and `BarCell` were verified visually in RTL, not measured.** |
| 2026-08-16 | T-116 · Every bar and every meter was painted in the warning colour | [2026-08-16-T-116-series-role-palette](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-16-T-116-series-role-palette.md) | Owner asked for colour variety across the charts. The answer turned on a finding: **`--sqx-chart-4` resolves to `--sqx-warning-darker`/`--sqx-warning-light`**, `BarSeries` defaults to `series = 1`, and `Gauge` **hardcoded** `CHART_SERIES[1]` — so every bar and every meter in the application was rendering in the token reserved for warnings, which is a status colour used as a series colour, a catalogued anti-pattern applied globally by default. **No token change request was needed**, and that is the reusable part: a **single-series** chart does not use the palette for *identity* — length carries the whole meaning — so the CVD-separation rule, which governs series *within* one chart, does not bind *between* charts. The only real constraint is contrast against the surface, measured for all four slots × both themes × both surfaces: lowest is **5.70:1** against a 3:1 floor, so this was a wayfinding decision rather than a contrast one. `SERIES_ROLE` now assigns a slot by **what the chart measures** — `volume` (info teal) for tallies: pipeline by status, operations states, active-by-state, today's states, workload bar cells, activity sparkline; `rate` (warning amber) for the `/analytics` rates band, left alone deliberately because it is an established screen and moving its hero colour is an owner call; `coverage` (AI violet) for measure coverage and `/analytics` metric coverage. `Gauge` gained a `series` prop defaulting to its old slot, so **no existing caller changed appearance** except the two passed explicitly. Verified by measuring rendered fills in both themes — dark `126,228,246` / `167,139,250` / `255,214,102`; light `33,92,102` / `109,40,217` / `122,65,0` — with **axe 0 violations on `/analytics` in dark and light after the change**. **Same task, second report: the `/analytics` rates band looked half-empty.** Measured before touching it, because two very different causes produce that look and only one is a bug — the chart root is **863px inside a 905px card**, so it was never a layout problem; the 494px gap **is** the unfilled remainder of a 0–100 rate scale. The obvious fix, rescaling to the largest value, would draw 34.5% as a full bar — precisely what `BarSeries`'s own TSDoc refuses as "exaggerating a flat run". `BarSeries` gained a **`track`** prop instead, painting the remainder behind each bar via Recharts' `background` with `CHART_TRACK`, the same token `Gauge` already uses for the same idea; the band now fills the card and the leftover reads as *scale* rather than *layout*. Verified in both themes — 5 tracks at 687px, `rgb(244,246,248)` light / `rgb(14,19,26)` dark, data bars unchanged, **axe 0 violations in both**. **Third report, same task: the two Breakdowns donuts were stacked**, because `analytics-breakdowns.module.css` declared `grid-template-columns: 1fr` at every width — half the card empty beside each. Now `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(20rem, 100%), 1fr))` with `align-items: center`; **the `min()` is load-bearing**, because this document already records that a bare `minmax(20rem, 1fr)` is a **320px reflow failure** — the track floor is a hard minimum and the item overflows its own grid below it (T-082). Measured: **960px → 419.5 + 419.5 on one row, card 271px tall; 768px → 323.5 + 323.5; 320px → stacked at 238, zero overflow.** `auto-fit` also means a single breakdown spans the full row with no conditional class. **RTL verified by measurement, not assumed** — first item at `left: 500` against the second at `56`, so the grid mirrors along the inline axis on its own; axe 0 violations in RTL. **Parked: `chart-5` and `chart-6` are `major` and `error` and must never become series colours; `chart-1` (brand) is unused by any chart and measures the highest light-mode contrast of the four (11.01:1), so it is the obvious fourth neutral slot if one is ever wanted.** |
| 2026-08-15 | T-115 · Two of the four remaining dashboard sections earned a chart; two did not | [2026-08-15-T-115-execution-state-split](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-15-T-115-execution-state-split.md) | Owner asked whether Today's operations, Execution status, Approvals and High-priority could become charts. **Two could, two could not, and the difference was in the data every time.** `activeField` was computed as a `.length` **with the filtered array discarded**, so the three governed states behind the number "9" were unreachable from the component — exposing `activeFieldRows` is three lines and no new query, and the split renders **executing 4 · on the way 4 · arrived 1**. Today's operations takes the same treatment and **self-gates to nothing**, because there are zero visits today. **Approvals was declined on data, not principle** — both tiles are 0 and `decidedScoped` is 0, so the decision mix (approved/returned/rejected, exactly three, the palette fit) is parked as the strongest unbuilt candidate rather than refused. **High-priority was declined outright:** `priority` splits high/critical, and a two-way split of **one row** is a single bar. **Overdue-by-age was considered and rejected on principle** — bucket boundaries like "1–3 days" would read as an SLA banding, and the governed one is `slaWarnAtFraction`; inventing bands is WEB-008 §2. `stateSlices` returns `[]` below two distinct states and the caller drops the chart entirely, so one bar never restates the tile above it and an empty plot never reads as a broken one — the gate `OperationsStates` introduced in T-113, now shared. The breakdown renders **inside** its own section under an `h3 subheading` rather than as a card below, because the owner named sections, not slots; **adding it in place took `operational-view` to 224 lines**, over the 200 budget, so the four group cards were extracted to `operational-groups` — which is where they belonged, the file was building view models and rendering them — landing at **129 + 117**. **axe 0 violations / 0 incomplete**, heading outline **0 skipped levels**, RTL labels measured clear (168–169 against bars at 176), digits Arabic-Indic, enum labels resolved through `visits.enum` with no new keys. v5 unchanged at 77; the one finding in a touched file (`metrics.ts:149`) is pre-existing — the diff touches lines 343, 345 and 502 only. |
| 2026-08-15 | T-114 · Latin numerals were leaking into Arabic everywhere a number skipped a formatter | [2026-08-15-T-114-arabic-numerals](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-15-T-114-arabic-numerals.md) | Owner-reported on the shell's unread badge; the audit found it across `/analytics`. **`toLocaleString("ar")` returns `58`; only `"ar-SA"` returns `٥٨`** — the bare `ar` tag defaults to `latn` numbering — so `analytics-counts.tsx` and `analytics-breakdowns.tsx` were calling `value.toLocaleString(locale)`, which *reads as locale-aware code* and silently emitted Latin digits. `metric-registry.ts` was worse: `count = v => v.toLocaleString("en")`, **hardcoded**, formatting every count on the route in English regardless of locale, with *"factories"*, *"inspectors"* and *"h"* as English literals inside the format functions. **`/analytics` measured 49 Latin-digit nodes in Arabic → 0** (the 10 that remain are `AN-AC-*` trace codes, machine identifiers, correctly Latin per WEB-013 §3); shell badge `55 → ٥٥`; and the subtitle stopped rendering raw ISO dates (`من 2026-07-17` → `من ١٧ يوليو ٢٠٢٦`). **The most dangerous thing in the task was two lines:** `view.ts` classified a metric with `kind: display.endsWith("%") ? "rate" : "count"` — sniffing the *rendered string* — so localising the percent sign would have **silently moved all ten rates into the counts band**; replaced with `isRateMetric(key)`, derived from the formatter each registry entry already declares. The formatter moved from `app/(app)/dashboard/dashboard-format.ts` (which the shell cannot import) to **`i18n/numbers.ts`**, the parallel of `lib/dates.ts`, and `Donut` gained a `display` slot rather than a locale, because a design-system primitive must not acquire one. **`٪٥٠` prefix kept deliberately** — ICU's percent style for `ar-SA` yields `٥٠٪؜`, sign trailing plus an invisible ALM, and moving it is a copy decision for the owner; written into the TSDoc so nobody "corrects" it. **Responsiveness swept at 320 px across `/analytics` and `/dashboard` × EN and AR: 0 px page overflow in every combination**, plus one real defect fixed — the inspector-capacity card passed a 60-character sentence to `CardHeader trailing` as a `StatusPill`, measuring **328 px against a 320 px viewport**; a pill is for a short label and WEB-014 §5 puts a sentence in `description`. **Parked: `/operations` leaks English landmark names into Arabic** (*"Operations map"*, *"Map"*) and carries a duplicate `region` name, both in the map layer. **Owed: e2e and a native Arabic review of 3 new keys.** |

Pre-existing, in a file dense with `t(key, "English")` legacy, so it was **recorded rather than rushed** (WEB-008 §5). **Everything was measured under a Planner session, and `/operations` gates five further sections by role** — `sla-kpi`, `workload-risk`, `timeline-history`, `queues`, `monitoring` all exist and none rendered; an Ops persona may change several verdicts. **Parked: workload bars and a `geoHistoryRows` sparkline, both available here with no new query.** |
| 2026-08-15 | T-112 · `/dashboard` gets a chart layer, and every Arabic bar chart stops overlapping itself | [2026-08-15-T-112-dashboard-chart-layer](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-15-T-112-dashboard-chart-layer.md) | Owner-selected widget set from a design review that judged **every** dashboard item before building anything: measure coverage, inspector bars, pipeline breakdown, activity sparkline. **The screen was computing breakdowns and throwing them away** — `operational.pipeline` held the per-status split behind the bare number **217** (cancelled 117 · published 52 · draft 40 · returned 8), and the largest segment turns out to be the **117 cancellations already printed two cards above**, a relationship the dashboard had never shown. **`/dashboard` can chart what `/analytics` could not:** it loads raw rows carrying `window_start` / `submitted_at` / `decided_at` / `occurred_at`, so a real daily series is derivable with no new RPC — the exact form T-111 correctly refused against `analytics_metric_snapshot`. **Every Arabic bar chart in the application was overlapping its own labels, and had been since T-111.** In RTL the SVG inherits `direction: rtl`, which inverts what `text-anchor: end` means, so category labels extended *rightwards* from the axis and ran under the bars — measured **label 168→203 against a bar starting at 176** on `/analytics` before this task touched anything, and the same on the value labels (`١١٧` at 669-682 against a bar ending 677). Fixed centrally in `bar-series.module.css` (`direction: ltr; unicode-bidi: isolate`), verified **7px clear** on both routes, LTR measured unchanged. Found by rendering, invisible to every gate. **The new widgets shipped Latin digits onto the Arabic page** (`50%`, `2 of 4`) beside the screen's own `٠`; `formatCount`/`formatPercent` were extracted out of `formatValue` — which already held the only correct implementation — and the **existing** raw sites went through them too rather than leaving the screen half-and-half (`٪50 → ٪٥٠`, `7 من 85 → ٧ من ٨٥`, all dashboards, deliberate and visible). After: **zero Latin-digit nodes** in `main` except seed inspector names. **axe 0 violations / 0 incomplete** on both views × both themes × LTR and RTL, including a **pre-existing `empty-table-header`** fixed by *extending the primitive* (WEB-014 §11.4) — `DataColumn` gained `headerHidden`, rendering the name in `sqx-visually-hidden` — not by working around it. **Three things were refused:** drill-through on the pipeline bars (`/planning` has no governed status filter and inventing one is WEB-008 §2), an inspector *utilisation* gauge (daily capacity is `Not configured`, so there is no denominator), and a daily trend on an unbounded scope (it renders a sentence instead, the same refusal `queryEnforcementTrend` makes) — **which means the sparkline is invisible on a cold load, because the default dashboard scope is unbounded.** `--sqx-space-14` was wanted and does not exist; `--sqx-space-13` was used rather than adding a token. Typography baseline **unchanged**, v5 **77 → 77 with none in any touched file** (the chain was already red). **Parked: `riskAttention` renders `Unavailable` while `metrics.ts` computes it** — factory count, visit count and ratio per band are all there, a wiring gap and the cheapest real chart left; a dashboard-wide coverage card (this meter covers only the register's 4 and 6, not the ~13 blocked across the screen); **`--sqx-chart-1…8` fails the chroma floor and, in dark, the lightness band — two checks T-111's TSDoc does not mention**, though its separation figures are correct and pass. **Owed: e2e, a native Arabic review of 31 new keys, the first-load number, and a re-run under Leadership — everything here was measured under a Planner session and several measures are role-scoped, not missing.** |
| 2026-08-15 | T-111 · `/analytics` gets a chart layer, and loses the data it was throwing away | [2026-08-15-T-111-analytics-rebuild](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-15-T-111-analytics-rebuild.md) | **Twenty-six identical KPI cards, ten of them reading "Unavailable"** — 38% of the grid was absence rendered at the weight of data, and `View governed records` appeared **26 times**, the most-repeated string on the page. Now **4 meters · 2 donuts · 3 grouped bar bands**; leaf nodes **207 → 96**, legacy classes **14 → 0**, `<main>` landmarks **2 → 1** (`page.tsx:26` had nested one inside `AppShell`'s), route file **120 → 50**, charts **0 → 11**. **`--sqx-chart-1…8` fails the categorical validator in both themes** — slots 4↔5↔6 measure **ΔE 3.0 deutan / 5.1 normal**, indistinguishable to every reader — so `CHART_SERIES` ships **three validated slots with the evidence in its TSDoc**, and widening it is a token change request (WEB-002 §2), never a code edit. **Recharts 3.10.1 was the owner's call after I recommended against it**; the tokens then carried theming with **zero JavaScript** — `fill="var(--sqx-chart-2)"` resolves `rgb(33,92,102)` light / `rgb(126,228,246)` dark — verified before anything was built on it. **Three forms were refused as dishonest:** line charts (the RPC returns one row per metric per period; `p_group_by` at lines 518-520 only *filters* which rows return, it does not group), a funnel (58 visits → 9 published are not stages of one cohort), and 2-slice donuts (a catalogued anti-pattern — a single ratio against its own track is a meter). Counts became **grouped small multiples scaled to each group's own maximum**, because eleven counts do not share a unit and 54 factories against 58 visits on one axis is the dual-axis error in disguise. **Bar-axis labels are SVG `<a>` elements** — Recharts cannot make a `<Bar>` focusable, so a custom tick keeps every drill target keyboard-reachable (focus lands, name reads "Visit volume 58", 3px ring). **The first rebuild deleted a working feature and had to be caught:** the entire filter form, all 26 drill links, the 10-row bottleneck list, every lineage code and every definition went, because "declutter" was read as "remove". The owner asked whether data had been lost, the audit said yes, and all of it returned — **ask what a screen does before deciding what it does not need.** **90 Latin words were leaking onto the Arabic page**, and they were not the legends: the **26 metric titles and 26 definitions** were hardcoded in `metric-registry.ts`, so every metric name and formula rendered English under `dir="rtl"`. All 52 moved to the namespace (163 keys × 2 locales, scripted for parity and no mixing) — **the Arabic needs a native reviewer, I wrote it.** Also extracted `useMediaQuery` out of `shell-scope-controls.tsx` rather than copying it, and gave `DateRangePicker` an `id` so a `Field` label can point at it. **Owed: axe, manual checklist, e2e, the first-load number (WEB-005 §8), and the Arabic review.** |
| 2026-08-14 | T-110 · `/execution` leaves the legacy sheets | [2026-08-14-T-110-execution-rebuild](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-14-T-110-execution-rebuild.md) | **The screen carried a permanent false alarm.** *"Submission service unavailable"* rendered **unconditionally** — no query, no prop, no data source — styled critical and announced `role="status"`, on every load, forever. Under WEB-002 §9 an unsourced claim is not a degraded state, it is a fabrication; deleted on the owner's direction. **The Arabic empty state named row-security policies** and was not a translation of the English — now split by cause: *nothing scheduled* routes to Planning, *nothing matches these filters* carries the scope count and a clear action. **A native `<dialog>` deleted the focus trap rather than migrating it** — `showModal()` via a ref callback removes 35 lines of `useEffect`, `document.addEventListener`, `querySelectorAll` traversal and `origin?.focus()` bookkeeping, making containment, Escape and focus restoration platform guarantees; **a native `<dialog>` gets no accessible name on its own**, so `aria-labelledby` is wired explicitly. **`showModal()` is the WEB-012 library-handoff exception, not a breach** — there is no React API for opening a modal. `RevampExecutionWorkspace.tsx` **397 → deleted**, replaced by seven components, largest 165; route file **80 → 34**; **~120 `copy(locale, en, ar)` sites → 0** against a new namespace of **137 keys × 2 locales**, verified by script for key parity and **no mixing in either direction**; non-null `!` **6 → 0**, `as never` **2 → 0**, `useEffect` **1 → 0**; three hand-rolled `Intl` helpers hardcoding `en-GB` replaced by `lib/dates.ts`, with day keys moving from `.slice(0,10)` to `riyadhDateParts` (DST- and tz-safe where the slice was not). **The coordinates fact rendered 5 times and now renders once**; two always-on banners gone. **WCAG 2.2 AA 2.5.7 closed** — every calendar entry now carries a Reschedule button, so the drag is no longer the only path; filter chips that cycled blindly became `Select` listboxes; status moved from `badge badge-*` to `StatusPill`. **The pinned spec was re-pointed, not deleted**: its 14 source-text greps asserted the *spelling* of the trap, and are now five browser tests asserting the claims — and because it `readFileSync`s the workspace at module scope, **deleting the file first would have thrown before any assertion ran** (T-078's lesson, avoided by checking). v5 gate **105 → 103**, route-owned **1 → 0**; rule sweep over all new code measures **0** for non-null, `any`, comments, `let`, `<svg>`, px/hex, feature typography, physical properties and legacy classes. **Owed: axe, manual checklist, Arabic render, e2e, and the first-load numbers** (WEB-005 §8 — the agent may not run a production build). **Parked: there is no `LocaleProvider`**, so `error.tsx` reads `documentElement.lang` via `useSyncExternalStore` — effect-free, but its server snapshot is `en`, so a server-rendered error shows one English frame in Arabic; **every `error.tsx` in the repo has this shape or hardcodes English, and a provider on `AppShell` fixes ~25 boundaries at once.** |

| 2026-08-14 | T-108 · the dashboard stamp was British, and only in English | [2026-08-14-T-108-dashboard-refreshed-stamp](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-14-T-108-dashboard-refreshed-stamp.md) | **The defect was a bypass, not a format.** `dashboard-sections.tsx:28` carried its own `Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-GB", …)` with `hour12: false` and time only, so **the Arabic dashboard rendered Latin digits in British format regardless of locale** — the same class of defect the typography sweep keeps finding, in the date layer. Fixed by routing through `lib/dates.ts`, the governed single source, rather than correcting the private copy. `en` **"20:28" → "14 Aug 2026, 8:28 PM (Riyadh)"**; `ar` **"20:28" → "١٤ أغسطس ٢٠٢٦, ٨:٢٨ م (الرياض)"** — Arabic-Indic digits, `م` marker, Gregorian calendar (`ar-SA` silently defaults to `islamic-umalqura` without an explicit calendar, which is why that file exists). **`formatDateTime` gained `{ hour12 }` defaulting to `false`, so all 94 existing call sites are byte-identical** — changing the default moves every timestamp in the app and is a design-system decision, not a dashboard one. **`hour12` also switches `hour` to `numeric`, measured against ICU rather than assumed**: `2-digit` renders `08:28 PM`, not `8:28 PM`. The toolbar now takes the raw instant and formats where `locale` already lives. **Parked: `formatDateTime` joins date and time with a Latin comma and Arabic takes `،`** — visible in the measured output, a WEB-011 defect, and fixing it moves all 94 consumers so it needs its own task. Also **`Text` cannot carry `dateTime`**, so every `<Text as="time">` in the app emits a `<time>` with no machine-readable value. |
| 2026-08-14 | T-107 · the typography gate went green on a file that did not compile | [2026-08-14-T-107-explain-panel-missing-import](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-14-T-107-explain-panel-missing-import.md) | `explain-panel.tsx` used `Heading`, `Mono` and `Text` across **nine call sites and imported none of them**, so `npm run typecheck` was **red on the branch**: 10 errors, all in one file. **`Text` failed loudly because it collides with the DOM lib's global `Text` interface** — TypeScript resolved the name and then rejected it as a component — while `Heading` and `Mono` failed quietly as unbound identifiers, which at runtime meant the explain popover threw a `ReferenceError`. One barrel import restored all nine. **It landed in `1bd7abdd refactor(typography): clear operations and explain-panel to zero`** — a commit that turned the typography gate green and shipped a file that does not compile. **This is T-102's lesson one level worse:** that task established the gate cannot see a rendered defect; this one shows **nothing in `npm run gates` typechecks at all**, so a non-compiling file passes every gate this repo has. **And `npm run verify` does not exist either** — `CLAUDE.md` requires it before any task is called done and the session template has checkboxes for `lint`, `unit` and budgets, but `package.json` carries **none of `verify`, `lint`, `test`, `unit`, `budgets`**. T-102 recorded the missing `lint`; it is actually all five. **The defect shipped because the one command that would have caught it is the one no rule names.** **Closed the same day:** `gates` is now `typecheck && gates:typography && check:design-system-v5`, typecheck **first** so the chain short-circuits before style-checking code that does not compile — proved by re-introducing the defect (exit **2**, style gates never run). **The first attempt at that proof was worthless and looked fine**: a `\n`-terminated replace against a **CRLF** file matched nothing, left the file untouched, and reported typecheck passing — T-090's zero-match shape, hit while building the control meant to catch this class of defect. **A test that injects a defect must assert the injection landed**, not read the exit code. Also corrected here: the v5 gate's real figure is **77 findings**, not the 103 quoted through this session, which counted bracketed tags and over-counted multi-line entries. `verify`, `lint`, `test`, `unit` and `budgets` remain absent. |
| 2026-08-14 | T-106 · axe on the topbar, and the compact gap filled | [2026-08-14-T-106-topbar-axe-and-compact-scope](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-14-T-106-topbar-axe-and-compact-scope.md) | **axe: 0 violations, 25 passes, 3 incomplete — and incomplete is not clean.** Each was resolved by measurement rather than waved through: `aria-valid-attr-value` is **not a defect** (lazy popup — proved `idExistsWhenClosed: false`, `idExistsWhenOpen: true`); the badge `color-contrast` **passes at 7.88:1**, flagged only because the badge overlaps the bell so axe could not sample the backdrop; but `duplicate-id-aria` was **real and critical — 7 duplicated `sqx-nav-*` ids**. **The rail renders twice** (`variant="rail"` desktop + `"drawer"` mobile) in one document, and subgroup ids were built from group + entry alone, so **every `aria-labelledby` in the drawer resolved to the rail's label** — seven `role="group"` regions named by an element in a different landmark. Ids are now variant-scoped: **7 → 0 duplicates across 14 ids**. Separately, filled the `compact` gap T-105 raised: `date-range-picker` and `select` gained opt-in `compact`, and `select` gained `icon` because an icon-only trigger showing a bare chevron names nothing. **`compact` is a prop, never a media query inside the primitive** — the shell decides via `useSyncExternalStore` over `matchMedia`, so **no `useEffect`**. The label moves into the accessible name rather than being deleted (`نطاق التاريخ — من ١٦ يوليو ٢٠٢٦ إلى ١٤ أغسطس ٢٠٢٦`), with a `PingDot` marking a carried value. Scope now survives from **1361px → 881px**, reclaiming 480px of viewport; 23 `Select` call sites unaffected since both props default off. |
| 2026-08-14 | T-105 · the header stopped starving its search, and the date scope became clearable | [2026-08-14-T-105-topbar-responsive-and-clearable-scope](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-14-T-105-topbar-responsive-and-clearable-scope.md) | **The search input measured 4px across the entire 900–1300px band** — `.scope` sized to its content and `.search` was the only flexible sibling, so it absorbed every shortfall; at 768px the scope was 368px inside a 204px parent and **painted over the action row**. Search now has a `min-inline-size` floor and the scope takes `flex: none`. **The breakpoint is measured against the scope's WIDEST state, and that is the lesson**: unset it is 220px, but carrying an Arabic range — `من ١٦ يوليو ٢٠٢٦ إلى ١٤ أغسطس ٢٠٢٦` — it is **357px**. A first attempt set it at 1160 against the unset state, measured clean, and spilled again the moment a date was picked — **exactly how the original overlap shipped**. It is 1360. **Search is no longer deleted below 480px** (it was the only control dropped while locale, theme and AI survived); below 560 it collapses to a touch-target icon and expands over the bar on `:focus-within`, so tap-to-open costs **no client state**. Separately, **Reset was a dead end by construction**: it cleared only the draft and never called `onChange`, while `apply` is disabled on exactly the state reset creates — a cleared range could not be committed by any sequence of clicks. Opt-in `clearable` fixes it for the shell; **none of the other seven picker call sites passes it**, so their behaviour is byte-identical. The header also stopped asserting a 30-day filter the URL never held. Verified at **seven widths × both directions** with the range set and unset: zero overlap, zero document overflow. |
| 2026-08-14 | T-104 · the shell stops charging every route for its typography | [2026-08-14-T-104-shell-typography](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-14-T-104-shell-typography.md) | **`components/app-shell/` 66 → 2, so every migrated route's shared floor drops 61 → 2.** Baseline **1846 → 1781**; shell renders 11 · 12 · 14 · 16 · 20px, all on scale, one typeface. **Half of `shell-topbar.module.css` was styling markup that no longer exists** — fourteen classes, **224 lines**, 14 of its 26 violations, superseded when the locale control became `SegmentedControl`, the user menu moved to its own module and the CTA became the icon-only `.aiButton`. **The user menu had an inverted hierarchy no gate could see**: `.name` was `label` (12px) under `.role` at `caption` (14px), so the role summary rendered *larger* than the person's name — fourth instance of the T-075 shape. **Geometry decided the correction**: the contract-literal §10 reading (both lines `body`) measures **54.4px in a 56px topbar in Arabic**, 1.6px clearance; `bodyStrong` over `label` measures 49.78px, and in English the block is **41.19px, identical to before** — the same two line boxes swapped. **`font: inherit` is legal in feature CSS** (matches neither gate rule) and is the only answer for an `<input>`, which has no children to host a primitive — **but what it inherits is the frozen legacy body rule**: both inputs measure 14px/**21px**, leading 1.5 from `saqeel-runtime.css:19` beating `saqeel.css:869`. **`tone="inherit"` is load-bearing in the rail**, where `.item` colour comes from `[data-state]`, `:hover`, `[aria-current]`, `[data-state="restricted"]` and a `:has()` parent repaint. **Two more already-semibold no-ops removed** (T-088's shape, now three instances). **A partial `replace_all` shipped a live regression**: `shell-nav-item.tsx` renders the same two lines at 8-space and 6-space indent, the replace matched one, and **every clickable nav item rendered at inherited 14px/400** while the gate stayed green — it counts declarations deleted, not text rendered. Fourth instance after T-058, T-076, T-090; **a partial match is worse than a zero match because it looks like success**. **`shell-page-frame` was deleted and restored** — zero importers read as dead, but `04-COMPONENT-LEDGER.md:180` says it supersedes the default `Shell` and awaits adoption in 55 route files, so **zero importers is its expected state; the ledger belongs in T-077's is-it-really-dead checklist**. `Avatar` and `CountBadge` were **rejected on inspection** (frozen-sheet global + inline style; a pill→`radius-sm` shape change inside a typography task) — **reuse is conditional on the primitive being clean**. `Kbd` adopted and its own retired `code` role fixed per §2.1. **Two violations survive deliberately**: the `صقيل` wordmark needs `subheading`, which `Text` cannot express and `Heading` can only reach by putting an `<h1>` on every page — raised as *is a logotype typography at all*, not filled inline. |
| 2026-08-14 | T-103 · the nested shell overflowed every route by exactly one topbar | [2026-08-14-T-103-nested-shell-viewport-overflow](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-14-T-103-nested-shell-viewport-overflow.md) | **93 route files mount the legacy `Shell` inside the layout's `AppShell`.** The legacy shell pins **three** boxes to a hard `100dvh` (`saqeel-runtime.css:540/:558/:722`) while `AppShell.main` is `100dvh` minus the topbar — so `.main` scrolled **exactly 56px** into the region below the inner shell, and `.sq-shell__main`, the only one carrying `overflow-y: auto`, ran its scroll range 56px past the visible box, leaving **the last 56px of every long page unreachable** and the scrollbar's own end under the clip.
