| 2026-08-11 | T-066 · `/dashboard` Operational View — the design's four metric groups (declutter part 3) | [2026-08-11-T-066-operational-view-four-design-groups](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-066-operational-view-four-design-groups.md) | **Partial — verified in EN and AR RTL from the DOM; axe, 320px and screenshots owed.** Owner reported the operational tab as cluttered, and **the design had already answered it — the shipped code had ignored one line.** `saqeel-revamp.html` defines `OPERATIONAL` as **four labelled groups** (Today's operations 2 · Execution status 2 · Approvals 2 · Operational exceptions 1) and the app rendered all seven metrics under a single "Today's operations" heading, which is **factually wrong for four of them**: returned reports and reports awaiting approval belong to Approvals, overdue visits and active inspections to Execution status. A supervisor scanning for approvals had no reason to look under "today", and **a heading that misdescribes its contents is worse than no heading.** The fix carried no functional risk because **the `today` array was already in the design's exact group order** — planned, completion, active, overdue, awaiting, returned, high-priority — so only the group boundaries had been lost: no query, metric, value or ordering changed, and the whole diff is composition. It also removed the visual defect the owner was actually looking at: seven cards in a `CardGrid min="md"` fit six at 1700px, stranding the seventh beside roughly **1100×180px of dead space**, the largest single element on the screen. Sections **1 → 4**, cards per section **7 → 2·2·2·1**, `h2` landmarks **4 → 7** — each now naming a real group rather than one heading covering seven unrelated metrics, which is what a screen-reader user navigates by. **The header sentence deleted here was one I had introduced.** T-062 folded "{high} high-priority visits are pending execution; {overdue} published visits are past their recorded window" into this header from the deleted priorities panel to avoid losing copy; sitting directly above `High-priority visits pending execution` and `Overdue planned visits`, it was provably redundant, so relocating it had been the wrong call and dropping it is the right one. `priorities.summary` deleted from both locales; the governance footnote survives on Operational exceptions because it is a claim, not data. `priorities.footnote` keeps its key name deliberately — renaming churns both locales for no visible gain. **Both specs T-062 wrote had to be re-pointed one task later**, since they asserted the very sentence this task deletes; they now assert the four **group headings**, with `Approvals` as `exact: true` so it cannot match "Inspection reports awaiting approval" or "Open Review & Approval". **A spec that asserts a sentence is hostage to copy; one that asserts structure is not.** **Filtered drills were raised, not taken** (owner ruling), with the blockers written down precisely: `/execution` accepts **no `searchParams` at all**, so two of the four duplicate "Open …" buttons cannot be filtered without rebuilding that route, and while `/planning` does filter, its `PLANNING_TABS` describe *visit planning status* where these cards count *inspection reports*, and the `priority` keys come from a DB lookup this workstation cannot read — guessing either would send the reader to a plausible **wrong** list, which is worse than an unfiltered one. Also recorded: **the typography gate now reports 31 violations removed and again invites a re-baseline, and it is still not this work** — those come from the concurrent `/factories` pass in the same tree, and T-066 changes no typography declaration. |
| 2026-08-11 | T-063 · Dashboard specs re-pointed at the shipped strategic surface | [2026-08-11-T-063-repoint-dashboard-specs-at-the-shipped-surface](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-063-repoint-dashboard-specs-at-the-shipped-surface.md) | **Done, suite not executed — every assertion proved against its source and the live DOM instead.** The owner pointed at one spec; **the rot was in three.** Grepping the retired `RevampStrategicView` strings found the same dead assertions in `dashboard-business.spec.ts:92-93` and `exec-hard-states.spec.ts:103`, and fixing one while knowingly leaving two identical failures would have left the suite red for the same reason. **Two of the six dead assertions were static, not runtime**, and a DOM-only fix would have sailed straight past them: `web-admin-m1-dashboard.spec.ts` reads source files as text and asserted `en/dashboard.json` contains two governance sentences that live **only** in the retired component — `grep -c` over the locale file returns **0** for both. **A spec that greps source can rot without the DOM ever changing**, and it fails in a way that looks like a copy regression rather than a stale test. **Nothing was deleted; each assertion was re-pointed to where its claim actually lives now.** "No quarterly series is inferred" became an assertion on **`STR-KPI-003`'s registry note** — "violations has NO issue-time column … cannot be produced without silently substituting submission time, which is prohibited" — and "No generated claim is shown until a configured provider" became **`STR-KPI-012`'s note** (`evidence_refs`, "Disabled until configured") plus the brief's idle line; `heading "Provider output withheld"` became `heading "Executive AI brief"` with its advisory pill and idle text. **The registry is the better home for a claim about what the platform refuses to compute:** a redesign can move a locale string, but it cannot move an immutable formula note, and these two assertions had already been broken once by exactly that. The copy assertions that remain are about reader-facing honesty, which is what a copy assertion should cover. `exec-hard-states` stays **bilingual** — its point is that governed absence reads as policy in both languages — and its Arabic needle was verified byte-for-byte against `ar/dashboard.json` rather than typed from memory. **Verification without a suite run: 13 automated checks** that every asserted string exists in its real source (including the advisory pill's middot being byte-identical to the locale file), then a live-DOM confirmation that each runtime target resolves to exactly **one leaf element**, so `getByText` cannot raise a strict-mode violation. **A trap recorded for the next agent:** with the Browser pane undisplayed the page does not composite, so `innerText` returns empty for genuinely rendered nodes while `textContent` is unaffected — three assertions looked absent until re-checked. Playwright composites, so it is an artifact of the probe, not of the specs. This also removes the last e2e dependency on `RevampStrategicView`'s copy, so nothing in the suite now pins that file's strings. |
| 2026-08-11 | T-062 · `/dashboard` delete the Operational priorities panel (owner ruling) | [2026-08-11-T-062-delete-operational-priorities-panel](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-062-delete-operational-priorities-panel.md) | **Done — verified in EN and AR RTL from the DOM; axe and 320px carried forward from T-061.** Closes the blocker T-060 raised and refused to decide unilaterally. The panel was a heading, a summary sentence carrying two live numbers, and a governance footnote — no control, and both numbers already rendered as two of the seven cards directly beneath it. **The panel went, the information did not:** the summary became the Today's operations header description and the footnote its `CardFooter`, so the reader loses a card surface and not one sentence. Operational sections **5 → 4**, card surfaces 25 → 24, and `operational.priorities.title` deleted from both locales — a key whose only consumer is gone is dead copy, the same call T-061 made on `trend.current`. **The interesting part is the spec update.** Simply dropping the two assertions would have left the contract silent about a panel that had been canonical, so `web-admin-m1-dashboard.spec.ts` now asserts three things — the heading has **count 0**, the priorities summary is visible, and the no-AI footnote is visible — stating both what was removed *and* that the governance statement survived the move, which is the part that actually mattered. `dashboard-business.spec.ts` is a screenshot-evidence test so its heading assertion became the summary assertion in place. Both use a **regex rather than exact text**, because the summary interpolates two counts and asserting rendered numbers would tie a layout contract to seeded data. **A deliberate refusal worth recording: `npm run gates:typography:update` was NOT run, and the improvement it offered to lock in was not mine.** The gate reported **7 violations removed since the baseline**, but `git status` showed **27 uncommitted files under `components/sections/factories/**`, `saqeel/definition-list/` and `app/(app)/factories/**`** — a concurrent typography pass sharing this working tree. Neither T-061 nor T-062 adds or removes a single typography declaration, so the delta was someone else's; re-baselining would have rewritten `scripts/typography-baseline.json` underneath them and locked a count taken mid-pass. **Read a gate's delta against `git status` before attributing it.** The ratchet only fails on additions, so the gate is green either way and whoever finishes that work owns the re-baseline. The same check also showed T-060 had been committed as `fae14075`, so the changed-file list handed back covers T-061 and T-062 only. **Parked with its trap recorded:** `DashboardSkeleton` never followed the executive brief to the top of the strategic view (`7c9fd7d3`), so everything below shifts by the strip's 44px when data lands — **but the skeleton must not be keyed on `scope.view`**, because `effectiveView(scope, isAdmin)` renders *operational* for a non-admin with no `view` param and the admin flag is only known after the very fetch the Suspense boundary is waiting on. Also parked: `MetricCardSkeleton` always draws a footer while blocked cards no longer have one, which may be unfixable rather than unfixed since blockedness is data-dependent at first paint, and `"1 high-priority visits"` has no plural rule in either locale. |
| 2026-08-11 | T-061 · `/dashboard` enforcement trend honesty, requirement register (declutter part 2) | [2026-08-11-T-061-enforcement-trend-and-requirement-register](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-061-enforcement-trend-and-requirement-register.md) | **Partial — verified from the DOM and computed styles in EN dark and AR RTL; axe, 320px and screenshots owed.** Owner screenshotted the lower strategic view, and the worst defect on the route was hiding inside a chart: **`enforcementTrendView` was scoring its own data.** It mapped a fall in penalty notices to `success` and a rise to `warning`, and the comment directly above the code claimed the movement "is a signal to read, not a score". Fewer penalty notices can mean improved compliance **or** reduced enforcement coverage — the screen cannot know which, and the **executive brief two sections up promises in as many words that it "does not attribute a cause"**, so the same screen was contradicting itself. The judgement was also applied to the wrong element: `tone` paints **every** bar in the series, so a decline rendered the *previous* period — the one with **more** enforcement — as a full-width green block, with the current period's zero as a thin green line beside it. The loudest thing on the route meant "six penalty notices happened, before now". Tone is now permanently neutral **with the reasoning written into the function's doc comment**, because a future agent will otherwise restore the colour as a nice touch. **Two further defects stacked in the same chart.** The approved design renders each bar column as bar → visible period label → visible value with tabular numerals; shipped, the dates lived only in `sqx-visually-hidden`, so **the sighted reading and the announced reading disagreed about which bar is now** — a screen-reader user knew and a sighted user guessed. And `.bar` floored every bar at `--sqx-space-2` (4px) **in the value colour**, so the current period's 0 rendered as a small green bar: an absence drawn as a quantity, on a route whose entire governance vocabulary exists to prevent exactly that. A zero point now renders as a 2px dashed baseline with a printed `0`. **The zero fix went into the shared primitive rather than the call site** on an owner ruling, so `factory-trends` inherits it — a recorded risk score of 0 was mis-drawing the same way. `TrendBars` took two **optional** props (`caption`, `value`) so its other consumer is untouched; chart height moved `--sqx-space-11` → `--sqx-space-13` toward the design's 120px and columns capped at `--sqx-space-13`, which is what stops a two-point series rendering as two half-width slabs. **No token was added** — a space token used as a size already had precedent in that same file. Requirement coverage collapsed **6 card surfaces → 6 `DataTable` rows** with every lineage drawer intact and now naming its own metric; **no "Live" pill was invented**, because `statusLabel("live")` is a hardcoded string in `dashboard-format.ts` and a live row can simply show its value in the State column. Blocked metric cards lost a genuinely dead call to action — "Top violated regulation → Unavailable" was still offering **Open the regulation**, and "Factories pending annual inspection → Not configured" offered **Open Planning**, each costing a divider, a padding band and a tab stop to lead nowhere. `trend.current` was **deleted from both locales**: with the count printed under its own bar, "0 this period" restated a number 40 px below it, and a key whose only consumer is gone is dead copy. **Deliberately kept:** "Top violated regulation" (*Unavailable* — no linked violations in scope) and `STR-KPI-003` (*Decision required* — the schema stores no violation issue date) look like one measure rendered twice and are two different facts with two different remedies. **A verification limit worth recording:** with the Browser pane undisplayed the page stops compositing frames, so screenshots time out and **every `getBoundingClientRect` returns 0** — computed styles and DOM structure are unaffected and carried the pass, but layout numbers cannot be read that way. Typography held at 1104 and `check:design-system-v5` at 91 pre-existing findings, both re-confirmed. |
| 2026-08-11 | T-060 · `/dashboard` duplicate KPI layer, AI brief strip (declutter part 1) | [2026-08-11-T-060-dashboard-duplicate-kpi-layer](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-060-dashboard-duplicate-kpi-layer.md) | **Partial — both views verified signed-in, EN dark and AR RTL; axe, 320px and e2e owed.** Owner reported the screen as cluttered with too many widgets, and **the cause was a regression this programme introduced**: the retired `DashboardView.tsx:513` passed `excluded={representedIds}` into its coverage grid, and the rebuilt `strategic-view` passed all twelve ids, so the "Strategic requirement coverage" block re-rendered **six measures already on the screen**. The worst of it is not layout: `STR-KPI-001` was rendering as "Compliance rate trend" in the strip *and* "National compliance rate" as a card, with the identical numerator and denominator — **two names for one governed figure on a ministry surface, where a reader cannot tell whether they are two measures that happen to agree.** Same for `004`, `006`, `007`, `008`, and `012`, which duplicated the AI brief while contradicting it (the tile said "Not configured" beside a working Generate button). `DashboardView.tsx:426` had also recorded *why* the blocked states were consolidated once before — *"repeated warning pills made disciplined absence read as a broken product"* — and that lesson had been dropped too. Both are restored in code. Strategic **22 → 16** tiles, requirement strip **12 → 6**, absence badges **14 → 11**; operational **20 → 15**, strip **9 → 4**. **`STR-KPI-003` was deliberately kept** against the first plan: the "Top violated regulation" card renders the by-regulation breakdown, which is available, while `003`'s blocked state is about the **time series**, which is not — two different facts about one metric, so excluding it would have hidden a governed absence rather than a duplicate. Enforcement trend moved below intervention, restoring the order both the approved design and `exec-overview-gate.spec.ts:10` state. The executive brief went **~182px → 44px** as the single-row strip `/planning` already uses, with provenance and the no-cause caveat rendering *with* a generated brief instead of stacked above an empty one — the top of the screen had been the loudest element on the page and the only one carrying no information. It copies that strip's layout but **not its CSS**, which declares `font: var(--sqx-text-overline)` in feature code against rule 7b. **`requirement.description` was shipping "Decision required: approved description pending." to both locales as a pinging warning pill** — internal task chatter presented to the reader as a status. **The two most valuable outcomes came from reading the e2e specs before editing, not after:** "Operational priorities" is an asserted **canonical panel** (`web-admin-m1-dashboard.spec.ts:217`, `dashboard-business.spec.ts:124`) so deleting it is a contract change and was raised instead of taken; and the planned "Your work" title swap would have broken **three** exact-heading assertions (`dashboard-business.spec.ts:200`, `:212`, `persona-tours.spec.ts:41`). The real header defect was the reverse — **`yourWork.scoped` was defined in both locales and rendered nowhere** while `:166` asserts "Scoped to your access" is visible, so the fix repairs an already-red assertion rather than creating one. **16 disclosure buttons shared one accessible name** ("How is this calculated?") plus an identical tooltip, because `Button` maps `label` onto both `aria-label` and `title`; each now names its metric. Blocked tiles sort last in every strip via one stable sort in the shared `MetricStrip`. **RTL verified properly:** the strip's `border-inline-start` computes to `border-right-width: 2px` under `dir="rtl"`, so the AI accent mirrors with no `[dir="rtl"]` override. Zero new tokens, zero new i18n keys, zero new gate violations — typography held at 1104 and `check:design-system-v5` at 91 pre-existing findings, both confirmed identical by stashing the diff and re-running. **Also found already red and untouched:** `web-admin-m1-dashboard.spec.ts:206` asserts a heading and body text that exist only in the retired `RevampStrategicView`. |
| 2026-08-11 | T-059 · `/dashboard` typography closeout, both views verified | [2026-08-11-T-059-dashboard-typography-closeout](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-059-dashboard-typography-closeout.md) | **Done — both views rendered signed-in and audited.** The first task in this sequence to actually open the screens in a browser, and it found **three defects two prior source-only tasks had missed**, which is the whole case for WEB-008's browser requirement. **(1) KPI numbers were rendering at two sizes on one screen — 30px and 28px.** `CardValue size="md"` still resolved through the retired `title` alias, which T-057 had mapped to `display`/30px, while the default used `metric`/28px. **This was the owner's original complaint — "see the font 81.5 it's so big compared to the rest" — still live after two tasks aimed at it**, because both call sites were perfectly token-clean and *a static gate cannot see that two valid tokens disagree with each other*. Only rendering shows it. Every number in the app is now `metric`. **(2) Table headers were 11px** while every other label was 12px, because `DataTable.head` used `overline`; WEB-014 §2 assigns column headers to `label`, fixed in the shared primitive so every table in the app follows. **(3) The page title had no font rule at all** — `.sq-pagehead__context > h2` in the frozen `saqeel-runtime.css` sets only layout, so the `<h2>` fell back to the **browser default 22px on every route**. That is the missing "typographic top end" diagnosed at the very start of this work, when `display` was measured at one use across the entire application; it now renders `Heading visual="display"`. Eleven dashboard components migrated off feature-CSS typography onto `Text`/`Overline`/`Heading`. **`tone="inherit"` was added to the type primitive** because a `<summary>` or button owns its own colour and nested text must defer to it — without that tone the only way to keep the interactive colour was to leave `font:` in feature CSS, so the missing tone was actively blocking migration. **The loading skeleton was corrected** — it still drew an eyebrow line above the title after T-058 removed the eyebrow from the real card, so the layout shifted when data landed. **A real inversion was fixed in the explain panel**, where the key rendered at 14px and its value at 12px, making the value smaller than its own label. `explain-panel` is otherwise left unmigrated (8 violations) because its `<h2>` needs `ref`/`tabIndex` on `Heading` for dialog focus return — parked as its own design-system task rather than made a third primitive change in one session. **Result: both views render the identical size set — 28 · 20 · 14 · 12, zero off-scale, one typeface — the first time that has been true.** Route debt 44 → 21 (13 of which are the dead `dashboard.module.css` awaiting deletion, not migration); repo 1,130 → 1,104. A buffered console `ReferenceError: Text is not defined at MetricCard` was chased down and confirmed **stale** — emitted during the HMR compile between adding the JSX and adding its import; the live DOM renders the full component with the primitive's generated class. **Owed:** axe, 320px, Arabic/RTL. |
| 2026-08-11 | T-058 · `/dashboard` — one typeface, title above subtitle | [2026-08-11-T-058-dashboard-typography-and-one-typeface](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-058-dashboard-typography-and-one-typeface.md) | **Partial — token and font verified in a browser; authenticated cards not viewed.** First screen of the screen-by-screen migration, and the finding outranked the brief: **the app was rendering four typefaces at once** — Segoe UI (224 elements), IBM Plex Sans Arabic (209), Times New Roman (4 unstyled), Consolas (every mono site). `--sqx-font-sans` read `"Readex Pro", "IBM Plex Sans Arabic", system-ui, sans-serif` and **neither named family was ever loaded**, because `next/font/local` registers a *scoped* family (`plexArabic`) that the token never referenced, so every token-styled element silently fell through to `system-ui` while elements using `--font-plex-arabic` directly got the real face. This is the worst class of design-system bug: **CSS raises no error for a missing font family — it just renders something else**, and a reviewer reading the token sees a correct-looking stack. Found by measuring rendered glyph widths on canvas rather than reading `computedStyle.fontFamily`, and that recipe is now WEB-014 §2.0 along with a ban on ever naming a family as a string literal. Fixed by pointing the token at `var(--font-plex-arabic)`, aliasing `--sqx-font-mono` to it (owner ruling: no second typeface — JetBrains Mono was never loaded either, so `mono` keeps only tabular numerals and weight), and declaring `font-family` on `:root` so nothing inherits the UA serif. Verified after: every rendered family resolves to `plexArabic`, and distinct sizes on the page fell **8 → 5**. **Owner ruling inverted WEB-014 §5 as written the same day in T-057:** the card title comes first and is always the larger and whiter of the pair; the description comes second at `body`/secondary. `CardHeader.eyebrow` renders exactly the pattern the owner rejected — a long question in 11px uppercase grey read *before* the title — so §5.1 marks it retiring behind a new gate rule, with **24 call sites** left to burn down. **KPI tiles were deliberately exempted** (§5.2, owner-confirmed): a tile whose subject is a number is label → value, and promoting "Visit pipeline" above 217 in size would demote the thing the tile exists to show — recorded so a future agent does not "fix" it. **The new gate rule initially matched 0 of 24 real call sites** because the pattern was line-scoped and every real `<CardHeader>` spans multiple JSX lines; adding a file-level predicate surfaced all 24. **A gate that silently matches nothing is worse than no gate** — new rules are now proven to fire before a pass is trusted. **`dashboard.module.css` was deliberately not migrated:** 318 lines carrying the worst literals in the repo (`52px`, `42px`, `34px`) on the pre-Saqeel `--type-*` set, but an import-graph walk showed it is reached only by `DashboardView`/`DecisionCanvas`/`RegionalScope`/`BasisDrawer`, and **nothing imports `DashboardView`** — the route renders `DashboardSections`. Migrating it was the largest chunk of work on the task and would have produced no rendered change; routed to retirement instead. All 10 retired-role references in `components/dashboard/` cleared; baseline re-levelled 1,140 → 1,130. **Zero new i18n keys** — both swapped strings already existed in `en` and `ar`. **Owed:** axe, 320px, and an owner pass on the authenticated screen. |
| 2026-08-11 | T-057 · Typography contract — nine roles, type primitives, ratcheted gate | [2026-08-11-T-057-typography-contract](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-057-typography-contract.md) | **Done — static and token-level verification; authenticated screens not viewed.** Owner reported from a `/factories` screenshot that prose rendered at two sizes inside one card, card titles varied, and eyebrow/title order was inconsistent. **The audit's central finding is that no rule was broken:** both named components (`factory-risk-outlook`, `factory-ai-advisory`) used only `var(--sqx-text-*)`, carried zero hardcoded values, and passed every gate — `.body` was `body` (14px) and `.meta`/`.note` were `caption` (11.5px), and the design system *permitted* that. The inconsistency was token-legal, so a stricter rule or better-behaved agents could not have prevented it. **The cause was choice.** Twelve roles with four inside a 2px band (10 / 11.5 / 11.5 / 12) and no selection rule, so agents defaulted to the smallest that fit: `caption` used **164×** against `body` 59×, `label` 68×, `overline` 33×. Measured across all 399 token consumptions, **72% of type usage sat at ≤12px and 3.5% above 16px** — the app had no typographic top end, which is the real reason it did not read as enterprise. Separately, **203 raw `font-size` declarations were already shipped** in direct violation of CLAUDE.md rule 7 (52px, 34px, 14.5px, 12.5px), which is why agents drifted at all: **precedent beat prose** — an agent reads the rulebook, then reads the neighbouring file, and the neighbour wins. Scale cut **12 roles → 9** with **one prose size**. `caption`, `body-lg`, `title`, `code` retired but **kept as resolving aliases** rather than deleted, because deleting them would have made `font: var(--sqx-text-caption)` unresolvable at 164 call sites and dropped the declaration to inherited type hours before a demo; instead **the alias performs the migration** and all 164 sites became 14px/1.6 in one edit, fixing exactly the defect reported. New `components/saqeel/type/` (`Text`, `Heading`, `Overline`, `Mono`, `Metric`, all Server Components, zero client JS) is now the only place permitted to declare a typography property — converting the unenforceable question *did the agent pick the right role?* into the greppable *did the agent write `font-size`?* **No `SectionCard` was built:** `Card`/`CardHeader` already enforced eyebrow → title → description, and the owner's "subtitle before title" complaint came from screens hand-rolling cards, so WEB-014 §5 makes the existing component mandatory instead. Card titles unified **16px → 20px**, the largest visual change here. `npm run gates:typography` baselines **1,130 violations across 380 entries** and fails any new one — a ratchet that may only go down; **proven to fail** by planting a probe violation and confirming exit 1, then removing it. `WEB-014` authored; `CLAUDE.md` rule 7b and the README index point at it. **Kept 14px body rather than the recommended 15px** purely for demo-night risk — `caption` → `body` already moves 164 sites up 22%. **Owed:** axe, 320px, and an owner eyeball of `/factories` and `/dashboard` for table reflow — sign-in requires credentials the agent does not enter. **Found pre-existing and parked:** `check:design-system-v5` was already failing before this diff (verified by stashing and re-running), and `/ar/login` serves `<html lang="en">`, meaning the `:lang(ar)` line-height overrides may fire on no route at all. |
| 2026-08-11 | T-056 · `/planning/single` first-run state, dead publish bar, step numbering | [2026-08-11-T-056-single-visit-first-run](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-056-single-visit-first-run.md) | **Partial — static verification only.** Owner reported the empty screen: one search card, then a raised sticky bar with **two permanently disabled buttons** and 600px of nothing. The bar now renders only when a target exists — `PublishReadiness` and `PublishBlockers` were already gated that way, so the bar was the outlier, and a disabled control is doubly wrong here because it leaves the tab order, so a keyboard user cannot reach the thing that would explain itself. **The fix has a trap: removing the submit button re-arms implicit form submission.** Per the HTML spec a form with no submit button submits on Enter when exactly one field blocks implicit submission, and on the empty screen the search box is that one field — the disabled button had been suppressing Enter by accident. `FactorySearch` and `PortfolioPicker` therefore moved **outside** the `<form>`, which is also what they are: selection, not submission. Safe because the published target has always been built from `TargetFields`' hidden inputs and never read back out of the radios — `target.ts` says so in as many words. **Cost, taken with an owner ruling:** `actions.ts`'s `factory_id` fallback, documented as backward compatibility for an older rendered form, is now unreachable; `license_number` and `location_confirmed` stay inside the form because the action genuinely reads them. The **permission blocker moved above the search** — it lived inside `PublishActions`, so gating that component would have hidden it until after a factory was chosen, and a planner without `planning.submit_for_supervision` should learn that before doing the work. **Two steps were both numbered "2 ·"** — `portfolioStep` "2 · Select the Industrial License / plant" and `licenseStep` "2 · Industrial License", in both locales. They are one step on two paths (canonical portfolio pick vs legacy licence confirm), so both now carry `Step 2 of 4` in a `CardHeader` eyebrow and the translated string holds only the name; numbers in copy are data in the wrong file. **The search input was named three times** — card heading, `TextInput`'s `label` (which writes `aria-label` only), and a placeholder restating the same four identifiers — against `TextInput`'s own TSDoc saying a control in a form belongs in `Field`. Now one visible `<label>` bound with `htmlFor`/`id`, one example identifier as placeholder. The minimum-length sentence went into the guidance `EmptyState` rather than `Field`'s `hint`, because `Field` renders its hint as an unassociated `<p>` with no id to point `aria-describedby` at, and giving it one is a design-system change needing a ruling (WEB-002 §2). Skeleton trimmed **3 cards → 1** to match the real first paint — pre-existing drift that this task made visible. **10 new keys in `planning.single` in both locales at asserted parity; no new `t(key, "English")`.** **Owed:** browser, keyboard (confirm Enter is inert), Arabic/RTL, axe — no dev server was started, and `npm run lint` / `npm run gates` still do not exist. **10 new Arabic strings need native review.** |
| 2026-08-11 | T-055 · `/planning` toolbar — filters into the panel, applied on change | [2026-08-11-T-055-planning-toolbar-instant-filters](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-11-T-055-planning-toolbar-instant-filters.md) | **Partial — static verification only.** Owner reported the bar "still looks cluttered", and the cause was structural: each filter rendered its name **twice** — once as a `Field` `<label>` and once as `Select`'s `aria-label`. **`Field` renders `<label htmlFor>` and `filter-controls.tsx` never passed `htmlFor`, because `Select` generated its list id internally and exposed nothing to point at** — so the visible caption was bound to nothing while the control named itself through `aria-label`. That is not a planning bug: **all 12 `Field` + `Select` pairs in the repo have it.** Raised as a gap and fixed only after an owner ruling (WEB-002 §2) — `Select` gained `id?`, puts it on the trigger and **drops its own `aria-label` when passed**, so the visible label becomes the accessible name rather than a second unassociated copy; `<button>` is a labelable element so `<label for>` binds. Additive, so the other 11 sites are untouched until each wires `id`. Toolbar then went **7 controls → 3**: search, Status, More filters, plus Clear all when active. The other nine filters moved into the panel and **every control names itself when empty** — `Status`, `Priority`, `Inspector` — which **needed no new i18n keys**, since the captions being deleted were exactly the strings the empty options now use. **Apply deleted at the owner's request:** filter state left `useState` for the URL, which is a rung *up* the WEB-004 ladder — the state had only ever mirrored the URL and been reconciled by a form submit — and each change is a `router.replace` with `page` cleared, `replace` not `push` so four tweaks are not four back-presses. **Filtering was already server-side and stays so:** `visit-list.ts` issues PostgREST `.ilike`/`.in`/`.range`/`.or` and flips embeds to `!inner` per filter; its own note records post-filtering being rejected for corrupting the row count. Instant-apply changes *when* the server is asked, never *who* filters. Owner also spotted Apply sitting 6px short — `size="sm"` is `--sqx-control-h-sm` (2rem) against every sibling's `--sqx-control-h-md` (2.375rem), bottom-aligned by `align-items: flex-end` so the gap opened above it; resolved by deleting the button and centring the bar. **Biggest risk, unverified:** `isOpen` lives in `MoreFilters` while every change navigates — React should preserve the client instance across a soft navigation, but **if it does not the panel slams shut after every selection and this is worse than Apply was.** Could not check: **the owner's Chrome extension is not connected**, so their incognito window could not be driven, and the in-app browser has no session. |

Hover is `--sqx-surface-subtle`, not `--sqx-surface-accent`, which WEB-009 §11 reserves for selection. **Three protections are not buttons** — AUTHORIZED, AUDIT and NOTIFY own nothing, and the legacy made them focusable controls that swallowed Enter; the roving tabindex went with them, since a `role="group"` of buttons is Tab-navigable by contract. **The announcement needed no effect:** it is a derived string, and React mutates that text node only when the string changes, which is exactly what the two `useEffect`s and a `useRef` were hand-rolling. `scrollIntoView({behavior:"smooth"})` deleted — it ignored `prefers-reduced-motion` and `focus()` already scrolls. **The blocking summary lists every blocker**, where the legacy told sighted users about one of four. CD-023's `LABEL — state — detail` name preserved on the button branch; every regex in that spec carries `/i`, re-verified before relying on it. |

The nine suggestion fields are now derived from `FIELD_REGISTRY` rather than restated. Every legacy class is gone from the surface (`sq-table` → `DataTable`, `sq-banner` → `PlanningNotice`, `grid-toolbar` → `Toolbar`, `sq-kpi-row` / `grid-footer` → their own components), **nine colour-only `sq-lozenge` became `StatusPill`**, and two emoji-as-icon went with them. **The table gained an empty state it never had** — `sq-table` rendered an empty `<tbody>`, which is exactly the defect the owner screenshotted. Session-storage handling moved to `features/planning-bulk/selection.ts`, removing the `exhaustive-deps` suppression; `Button.busy` replaced the last hand-rolled pending label. `BulkForm.tsx` marked `@retiring` at zero importers. Two owner-reported defects fixed after: the filter field collapsed to content width (`Toolbar`'s `.lead` is `min-inline-size: 0` and `Field` carries no width, so `TextInput`'s `100%` resolved against nothing — fixed with a `min-inline-size` wrapper in the screen, not by changing the primitive), and filtering gave no feedback. **The busy state is `useDeferredValue`, not a timer** — the input stays responsive, the filter runs against the settled value, and the gap between the two *is* the signal, so the indicator cannot lie. Table dimming was dropped rather than invent an opacity token (WEB-002 §2, the same call T-048 made). Fourth report — "why does the filter return nothing" — was **not a filter bug**: the table had no rows because no criteria were applied, and the single empty state said "adjust the criteria above", which describes a different failure. Split into a `TableEmptyReason` union of three (`noCriteria` / `noMatch` / `noFilterMatch`), each with its own copy and registry icon, derived from `criteriaApplied` and the pre-filter row count. `TargetingLensClient` computes `hasCriteria(initialTree)` itself rather than taking a seventeenth prop. **A screen that has to be explained in chat has an empty state that is lying.** Fifth report: the skeleton touched the rail and the viewport edge. `loading.tsx` returned the skeleton bare, so it never entered `.sq-content`, and the page head was missing during load then appeared — an inset bug and a layout jump from one omission. Both sibling routes already wrap their skeleton in `<Shell>` with the page title; doing the same fixes both. **A skeleton is only honest inside the frame the real screen renders in.** **The review step (`review/page.tsx` 288, `ReviewClient` 853) is still untouched** — slices 3 and 4. |

| 2026-08-10 | T-044 · nested menu dismissal scope | [2026-08-10-T-044-nested-menu-dismissal-scope](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-10-T-044-nested-menu-dismissal-scope.md) | `removeChild` of null when a `Select` opened inside a portalled `MenuSurface`. **Both portal to `document.body`, so a nested panel is a DOM sibling** however deeply nested in React — `contains()` returned false, the ancestor dismissed itself at `pointerdown`, and tore out the option while React was committing the inner portal's removal. | 2026-08-10 | T-042 · narrow the PostgREST boundary | [2026-08-10-T-042-postgrest-narrowing-boundary](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-10-T-042-postgrest-narrowing-boundary.md) | **All 48 `as unknown as` casts deleted from the migrated data layer**, replaced by one narrowing boundary (`lib/postgrest/{shape,read}.ts`) and a `Shape<T>` per row type across 7 domains. **The casts were hiding a real hole:** `supabaseServer()` carries no `Database` generic, so `.select()` infers every column as `any` **and every embedded relation as an array** — including to-one embeds PostgREST returns as objects. The hand-written row types were right about cardinality and the inferred types were wrong, so every cast silently bridged both problems at once, and a renamed column produced no type error and a runtime failure inside a component. Reads now **fail closed**: a malformed row fails the whole read rather than being dropped, because silently skipping rows would under-report a governed figure — absent data renders as a state, never as a smaller number. `hydrate.ts`'s six casts disappeared for free once `VisitScopeRef` declared the `factory_id` the query has always selected. Logging collapsed to one site (`[postgrest] <source> unreadable — <reason>`), taking `console.*` in `features/**` from 42 → 22. `typecheck` clean repo-wide. **150 casts remain in unmigrated legacy** — the boundary now exists for each screen migration to convert its own reads onto. **Open:** generated Supabase database types would make most of `features/*/shapes.ts` redundant; needs DB access this workstation lacks. |

| 2026-08-10 | T-039 · shell rail hydration on rewritten routes | [2026-08-10-T-039-shell-rail-hydration](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-10-T-039-shell-rail-hydration.md) | Two React reports on two routes, same shape: the server rendered `aria-current="page"` and the client rendered nothing. **The server resolved the pathname into alias space — the space every nav href is written in — and the client did not.** The only paths where those spaces differ are the three the middleware rewrites, which is exactly the affected set. The rewrite table lived inline in `middleware.ts` as a ternary chain, so nothing else could know a path had been rewritten; it is now `lib/route-rewrites.ts`, read **forwards by the middleware** (`designRouteFor`) and **backwards by the shell** (`shellRouteOf`). `shell-nav-group.tsx` runs both the server pathname and `usePathname()` through it, so they agree whichever space each reports — structural, not a guess about which side was wrong. Rejected the conventional defer-until-mount fix: it needs `useState`+`useEffect` and leaves the rail briefly wrong on **every** route. Middleware behaviour byte-identical, query guards included; non-rewrite paths untouched. **This was an accessibility defect** — `aria-current` was announced by the server HTML then silently withdrawn at hydration. Typecheck clean, mapping verified across alias/design/unrelated inputs; **not observed in a browser**, so the warning disappearing is unconfirmed. |

| 2026-08-09 | T-029 · `/factories` view-model extraction + Factory profile card | [2026-08-09-T-029-factories-profile-and-extraction](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-029-factories-profile-and-extraction.md) | **Did the blocking extraction first.** T-028 left `RevampFactory360Portfolio.tsx` at 361 lines against a 200-line limit (ceiling 400); adding the requested card first would have breached it. String maps are **typed against their consuming components** (`PortfolioStrings`, `FactoryContextStrings`, …), not `Record<string,string>`, which would have compiled while silently dropping a key. **Then the Factory profile card**, first in the disclosure stack, reusing `factory-sections`' chrome so it matches the four beneath it: Activity, Region, City and the primary active `factory_representatives` contact (with role, and "No active representative is recorded" when absent) are real; **Sector shows "Not available"** — no such column exists. **Media is counts, not thumbnails:** `factory_media_assets.storage_path` needs a signed retrieval surface that does not exist on this screen, and an `<img>` with no working source is a broken image, not a placeholder — the card reports "3 official factory images", says previews come from the factory profile where access is checked per asset, and links there. **Corrected after owner review:** the card rendered one fact per row and looked half empty — the disclosure chrome copied from `factory-sections` carries `align-items: flex-start`, which **shrink-wraps every child to its content width**, so `DefinitionList`'s `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(…, 1fr))` had nothing to fill and collapsed to one column regardless of card width. Removing that declaration fixed it; the four groups now sit in a `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(--sqx-grid-min-sm, 1fr))` grid that reflows, with a full-width `Button block` spanning beneath. **The trap is in the copied chrome, not the card** — `align-items: flex-start` is right for the four disclosures (a note and a button) and wrong the moment a disclosure holds a grid; anything else reusing those styles inherits it. Typecheck clean repo-wide, zero design-system findings, 144 i18n keys at parity. **Static verification only. Noted: `/factories` now issues eight reads per load across three batched groups — worth a measurement pass once it can run.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-028 · `/factories` risk trend + disclosures moved last | [2026-08-09-T-028-factories-trends-and-order](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-028-factories-trends-and-order.md) | Trends added under penalties; the four disclosures moved to the very end. **New order:** hero → snapshot → compliance (reports, violations, penalties, trends) → disclosures. The **risk trend is real** — up to six recorded `factory_risk_snapshots`, oldest first, with the delta against the previous calculation; a single snapshot reads "First recorded calculation", never a fabricated delta. The **compliance trend renders "Not available" with the reason**, because no compliance score exists for a factory — kept as a titled block rather than dropped, since here the absence *is* the answer (unlike a fact row that can only ever say "Not available", which is noise). **The chart is data, not decoration:** bars are an `<ol>` where each carries a visually-hidden label with its recorded score and date, so the series reads without seeing it; height comes from `--sqx-trend-value`, a **bare number** consumed in `calc(… * 1%)` — the same shape `SegmentedControl` uses for its index, so the component supplies data and never a length, and no visual literal enters a component. Scores are charted on their governed 0–100 scale rather than normalised to the series max, which would make a flat low-risk history look dramatic. Tone is derived: rising→danger, falling→success. Disclosures extracted to `factory-sections` (ordering them after compliance was impossible while they lived inside `factory-overview`), which drops to 75 lines. Zero new queries — the existing snapshot read widened from 2 rows to 6. Typecheck clean repo-wide, zero design-system findings, 127 i18n keys at parity. **`RevampFactory360Portfolio.tsx` is now 361 lines against a 200-line limit (ceiling 400) — the next `/factories` task must be the view-model extraction.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-027 · `/factories` compliance section | [2026-08-09-T-027-factories-compliance-section](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-027-factories-compliance-section.md) | The reference's Compliance block — inspection reports, violations, penalties — on three canonical `DataTable`s at the end of the middle column. Reports use the governed **`visits.visit_reference`** (`inspections` has no reference column, only a UUID — showing eight hex characters as a report number would invent an identifier), with `inspections.status` and `reviews.decision` as **separate columns** because they are different axes and the reference collapses them. Violations carry `violation_codes.title/code/level`. **Two reference columns were dropped rather than faked:** the violation `Open`/`Closed` pill (no such state exists — `invalidated_at` means *retracted*, not *resolved*, and mapping it to "closed" would report retracted violations as resolved, a materially wrong compliance claim; a caption states this) and `Fine — SAR 4,000` (`penalty_notices` has no amount column). **Found and fixed a live correctness bug in the making:** `penalty_notices` is RLS-restricted to reviewer/ops/auditor/compliance_admin/leadership, and every other role gets an **empty result, not an error** — so a planner would have read "No penalty notice has been issued", a false statement about the record. The query now returns `penaltiesReadable` and the section renders a `restricted` state instead. **An empty result under RLS must never render as an absence of facts.** That also exposes the same bug in T-024's Active-penalties tile, which shows `0` for those roles — recorded. Typecheck clean repo-wide, zero design-system findings, 115 i18n keys at parity. **Static verification only — none of the four reads has executed. Flagged: `RevampFactory360Portfolio.tsx` is now 336 lines against a 200-line limit (ceiling 400) and is mostly view-model construction; extract before the next slice.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-026 · `/factories` middle column — snapshot hero + header facts | [2026-08-09-T-026-factories-middle-column](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-026-factories-middle-column.md) | Third `/factories` slice. New `factory-snapshot`: an overall-condition panel plus a metric grid of **six real figures** — risk score, latest inspection (new read: `inspections.submitted_at ?? started_at` via `visits.factory_id`), open violations, active penalties, employees (`employees_total`), products (`factory_products` count). **Compliance rate and machines are dropped, not stubbed** — neither exists in the schema at all, and the owner's start-panel precedent is that a slot which can only ever read "Not available" is noise. **The condition reasons are derived, not written:** the reference hard-codes "Fire Alarm test expired / 104 days since last visit"; ours compute open-violation count, days since last inspection (or "never inspected"), and licence expired/expiring — and say so plainly when nothing is raising the condition. Header gained the reference's 4-up fact row (plant number, licence type, stage, status). **As much subtraction as addition:** removed the standalone provenance card (the end panel already carries it — the same three lines twice on one screen), the condition card (its facts were the risk score/band now in the snapshot plus two permanent "Not available" rows), and the snapshot-facts card (code/CR/region/city were already in the hero sub-line). Create Inspection / View on Map stay once, as asked; `Export PDF` **not** added since there is no export to call. `factory-overview` 159 → 104 lines; nine orphaned string keys and two dead CSS classes removed. **Corrected after owner review:** the metric grid was broken two ways — tracks sized from a *spacing* token (`auto-fit, minmax(6rem, 1fr)`) produced a six-column row far narrower than any label, and **every value used the 28 px display metric role**, so "Never inspected"/"Not available" were wider than their track and spilled across neighbours. Fixed with a **fixed column count** (`repeat(4, minmax(0,1fr))`, two at narrow — no length token needed) plus a `kind` on `FactoryMetric`: `number` keeps the metric role, `text` drops to `body-strong`/secondary. A `null` metric now renders as `text`, so an absent value stops being the loudest thing in the card. **Also, owner request: the whole type scale is now net −1 px per role** — applied as −2 px then +1 px after seeing it rendered, touching only the `-size` tokens so line heights, weights and tracking keep every role's proportions. Typecheck clean repo-wide, zero design-system findings, 95 i18n keys at parity. **Static verification only — the two new reads have never executed, and `factory360-provenance-contract.spec.ts` asserted against the provenance block this task deleted, so it is very likely red. Type-scale note: the rebase leaves the smallest roles at overline 10 px and caption 11.5 px — tight but defensible; and the recorded contrast ratios predate the change — `subheading` at 16 px semibold no longer qualifies as WCAG "large text", so pairs justified at 3:1 there now need 4.5:1.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-025 · `/factories` end panel — risk explanation + AI advisory | [2026-08-09-T-025-factories-end-panel-ai](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-025-factories-end-panel-ai.md) | Second `/factories` slice: the end panel now carries the mock's AI sections, built only from data the platform records. **"Why high risk" reuses the existing `FactoryRisk`** (score, band pill, model version, recorded driver breakdown) rather than growing a second one — the mock invents driver specifics we already hold for real in `factories.risk_drivers`. **"Latest changes"** comes from the two most recent `factory_risk_snapshots` ("Risk moved from X to Y on <date>") — two, because a movement needs a before and an after and anything further would be a trend the engine has not published. **The AI advisory reuses `generateContextualInsight`'s `factory_risk_explanation` surface**, whose prompt is already constrained to explaining the recorded score and drivers and forbidden from recalculating risk or recommending enforcement; `factory-ai-advisory` is presentation only, with `accent="ai"`, the advisory pill, the no-calibrated-confidence note, and fail-closed behaviour without a key. **"Predicted risk" — the mock's "projected 87–90 next cycle" — is the single most tempting fabrication and renders "Not available" with the reason**, because no forecasting model exists. **Data sources are two honest states** (Senaei synced / risk engine calculated) rather than the mock's three unconditional green ticks — a tick that is always green tells the reader nothing. `toDriverLines` reconstructs nothing: a driver missing a part prints the missing marker for that part. **Corrected after owner review:** the first pass scattered the mock's last two blocks into other cards (predicted risk as an AI-card footer, data sources as a provenance tail); they are now **cards in their own right, above the AI one** — `factory-risk-outlook` (`PREDICTED RISK` · `WHY THIS RISK` with score/band/drivers/model version/latest change · `NEXT BEST ACTION`) and `factory-trust` (`LAST SYNCHRONISATION` · `DATA SOURCES`). The duplication that would have caused was **removed rather than tolerated**: the outlook card absorbs the separate `FactoryRisk` and latest-change cards, so no section is stated twice and the panel is five cards. `FactoryRisk` therefore keeps its single `/factories/[id]` consumer and its Rule-of-Two promotion note is withdrawn. **`NEXT BEST ACTION` is navigation, not advice** — the mock writes a specific instruction ("review the fire alarm violation"); we have no such finding and inventing one would be the fabrication this panel avoids everywhere else, so ours points at the factory profile, which is true regardless of what the record holds. **`Button` gained `variant="link"`** — a text action for prose with no fill and no inline padding, so its label sits on the same start edge as the paragraphs above it and hover underlines rather than painting a block in the middle of body copy. Added rather than changing `tertiary`, whose hover fill is correct in the control rows it is used in everywhere else (planning filter bar, visit scope bar, quick actions); both AI advisories adopted it. Four stale string groups and two orphaned CSS class sets deleted. Typecheck clean repo-wide, zero design-system findings, 87 i18n keys at parity. **Static verification only — this advisory call site has never invoked the provider.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-024 · `/factories` start panel — real portfolio facts | [2026-08-09-T-024-factories-portfolio-panel](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-024-factories-portfolio-panel.md) | First slice of replacing vendor-mock content on `/factories`, **start panel only**. Every figure the mock shows was traced to the schema first: `PORTFOLIO — CR` / Factories / High Risk / Licence / Plant / Type / Stage / `Valid` pill were **already real**; **Compliance % has no column anywhere** and was **removed rather than stubbed** (a row that can only ever say "Not available" is noise); **Open Violations** and **Active Penalties** had no governed definition and are now **computed on owner-agreed rules** — violations reached via `inspections → visits` with `invalidated_at is null` (the nearest thing to "open" the schema has), and `penalty_notices` with status `issued`/`served`. **Licence expiry** is now a real fact plus an `Expired`/`Expiring soon` pill at a 30-day threshold, exported as `LICENCE_EXPIRY_SOON_DAYS` with a TSDoc note that it is a **display rule only** — no planning or enforcement logic reads it. Counts fail independently (`openViolationsAvailable`/`activePenaltiesAvailable`); a failed read renders "Not available", never `0`. **Second pass — test data removed, not just its label:** the screen is going in front of real users, so seeded rows must not appear at all. There were **two independent test signals and only one was applied** — `isTestFixtureEstablishment` (e2e fixtures by name/code) was filtering, while source-marked test data (`saqeel_test_data` / source containing `"test"`) was only being *labelled* by `provenanceOf`, never excluded. New `isTestSourceFactory` now filters **both the scope projection and the portfolio query** — without the scope filter a test CR would still have appeared in the chooser after its rows were hidden. `isEmpty` covers an emptied portfolio, so a CR whose rows were all test data shows the "no factories" state instead of the blank column the panel used to leave. The **four stats became one card** (overline `PORTFOLIO — CR`, 2 × 2 `<dl>`, `column-reverse` so the count reads above its label while the DOM keeps `<dt>` before `<dd>`), and counts carry a **tone only when non-zero** — a zero high-risk count is good news and must not be painted red; the label always carries the meaning, so tone is redundancy, never the signal. Licence status/expiry/risk moved from fact rows into a footer pill row (text plus shape); dates go through `formatDate(locale)`, not raw ISO. Skeleton updated to mirror. Typecheck clean repo-wide, zero design-system findings, 61 i18n keys at parity. **Static verification only — and the two new queries have never executed; the two-level `!inner` embed filter is the one thing that cannot be checked without a database.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-022 · Planning assistant — insights, recommendations, quick actions, stats | [2026-08-09-T-022-planning-assistant-panels](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-022-planning-assistant-panels.md) | Landed the vendor mock's AI band + stat row on `/planning` as six section components (none over 62 lines) on a two-file `features/planning/**` data layer. **Four of the mock's numbers are not governed data and were not copied:** "92% confidence" / "Confidence 96%" (Gemini returns no calibrated confidence — the platform already records `confidence: null, confidence_status: "provider_not_supplied"`), the per-visit "AI Score", "Needs Planning" (no such `planning_status`), and "Expiring Windows" (needs an SLA threshold). Owner ruling: recommendations rank and label by the **recorded `factories.risk_score`**, and the two undefined buckets render **"Not configured"**. **No edge functions were written** — investigation found the whole governed contract already exists (`ai_suggestions`/`ai_events` docket with mandatory human disposition and "AI never writes a decision", the fail-closed Gemini adapter, and a server action that re-reads under the caller's RLS before prompting, already carrying a `planning_summary` surface). An edge function would have re-implemented JWT + RLS + docket write **outside** those guardrails; owner chose to extend the existing path, so nothing needs deploying. `planning-ai-advisory` is a thin client island over that same action — new presentation, zero new logic; without a key it renders the neutral "provider unavailable" message. "Generate Weekly Plan" links into `/planning/bulk` rather than having AI create visits. "Assign unassigned visits" was **dropped** — the count is computable but no filter exists, and a quick action that links somewhere other than its label promises is worse than none; replaced with the real, filterable "awaiting supervisor". Every figure honours `countsAvailable` — a failed read shows "Unavailable", never `0`. Skeleton extended to mirror the new band. **Corrected after owner review:** icon moved from `eyebrow` (which stacks above) into the `title` node so it sits adjacent — also dropping an `Icon label` that made it announce as an image repeating its own heading; insight counts became `CountBadge superscript` (the T-021c treatment, so a count looks the same everywhere); the grid went `align-items: stretch` with `display: grid` columns so all three panels end at the row baseline instead of leaving dead space under the shorter two — done in the section, since a primitive takes no class from outside; the risk-band pill now goes through the common `enumLabel` (`sentenceCase(t("enum.x", humaniseEnum(x)))`) instead of printing the raw `high`; and **`Card` gained `accent="ai"`** — a stroke only on `--sqx-accent-ai` (both themes), fill left neutral because tinting the surface would make advisory content read as a status. **Quick Actions deliberately does not get the accent or the sparkle** (it uses `workflow`): it is deterministic navigation, and marking it AI would defeat the accent's purpose. **Quick-actions second pass:** "Build a bulk plan" and "Create a visit" were rendering **"Unavailable"** — a defect of my own making, because `count: number | null` had `null` doing two jobs (*this action has no count* vs *the count failed to read*); the type is now `count?: number | "unavailable"`, and a state that means two things eventually renders the wrong one. "Create a visit" became a **`MenuSurface` menu** over the same three governed methods `CreateVisitSection` already offers (bulk/single/immediate — same titles, descriptions, routes, so they cannot drift), living inside `planning-quick-actions/` to share its module; it inherits outside-pointer close, Escape-with-focus-return and viewport-aware placement, uses registry icons instead of that component's emoji glyphs, and renders a blocked method as a disabled `menuitem` with its reason. Rows gained leading icons and a taller target. **Third pass — two primitive bugs:** the create menu was **clipped by the shell's scroll container** (`.sq-shell__main { overflow-y: auto }`) — my first fix (a block-axis flip) was wrong, because an absolutely-positioned element *cannot escape a clipping ancestor*; no z-index or flip could have helped, and `Card`'s hover `transform` was a second trap that would have re-anchored a plain `position: fixed` panel. `MenuSurface` is now **portalled to `document.body` and fixed** from the trigger's viewport rect, keeping the above/below flip, clamping to the viewport, and re-running on capture-phase scroll; `--sqx-menu-start` is measured from whichever edge `inset-inline-start` resolves to, so one declaration serves both directions and `align="end"` hangs from the left in RTL. **Keyboard consequence handled:** a portalled panel sits at the end of the body, so `trapFocus` now also *moves* focus into the panel — a trap with nothing in it holds nothing. **This changed every menu in the app** (select, date-picker, date-range-picker, shell-user-menu, create menu); only the create menu was looked at, the other four need a browser pass. And the **AI accent vanished on hover** because `.root:hover` repaints `border-color` at *equal specificity* and later in source — `.root[data-accent="ai"]:hover` now holds the stroke and darkens the surface with `--sqx-surface-accent`. Also migrated **`CreateVisitSection`'s emoji glyphs** (`▦ ▣ ⚡`) to registry icons, which closed the method-list duplication parked the pass before: the Create panel and the new menu now derive from one `features/planning/create-methods.ts` and cannot drift. **43 `emoji-as-icon` findings remain** on un-migrated planning sub-routes. Typecheck clean repo-wide, zero design-system findings, 309 i18n keys at parity. **Static verification only. Flagged: the route file is now 555 lines against a 40-line cap and this task made it worse by ~75 — slimming it is the next task.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-021e · Planning skeleton + segmented-control width | [2026-08-09-T-021e-planning-skeleton-and-segment-width](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-021e-planning-skeleton-and-segment-width.md) | **`SegmentedControl` width fixed at the base.** Its root is `inline-grid`, which reads as "size to content" but is not that for a **flex or grid child** — those parents blockify and stretch, so the control sat inline inside a toolbar (`operations`, `dashboard`, `visit-scope-bar`, topbar locale toggle) yet spanned the full width when placed on a page directly, which is what `/planning` does. `inline-size: fit-content` makes the intent hold in every parent; all five consumers checked, none wanted full-bleed and none changes shape. Rejected wrapping the nav in a `Toolbar` at the `/planning` call site — that fixes one page and leaves the trap armed. **`/planning` loading state** went from the shared `RouteLoading` (a centred glyph inside its own nested `<main>`, mirroring nothing) to `planning-skeleton`, reproducing the real first-paint order: view toggle → heading + subtitle → right-aligned action row → filter bar → table card with shaded header band and 8×7 rows → count/pagination footer. The create-method grid is deliberately **not** drawn — it is collapsed on first paint, so drawing it would mirror a state the page never loads in. Built on `Card`/`Stack`/`Skeleton`/`SkeletonRegion` only, zero client JS, matching the dashboard/factories/visits skeletons. **Corrected after owner review:** the first cut stacked the heading and floated the actions right, because I built it from the JSX tree — but `/planning` still gets its layout from the frozen sheets, where `.sq-planning-heading` is `space-between` (title and subtitle share **one row**) and `.grid-toolbar` is a **bordered bar with actions from the start edge**. Toolbar bones are now `repeat(auto-fit, var(--sqx-space-13))`, not a percentage, since a percentage scales "buttons" with the viewport. **A skeleton for a part-migrated screen must be read from the CSS, not the component tree** — `/planning/bulk`, `/reviews` and `/field` are in the same position. `RouteLoading` **not** retired — 10+ admin routes still use it. Typecheck clean repo-wide, zero design-system findings, i18n parity (planning 262, common 39). **Static verification only — the segmented-control change touches five screens and wants an LTR+RTL pass, since the control carries a mirrored sliding pill.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-021d · Shared date presets, visit-status pill, ping geometry | [2026-08-09-T-021d-shared-date-presets-status-pill-ping](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-021d-shared-date-presets-status-pill-ping.md) | **Presets:** three call sites had three vocabularies (shell 7 inline, `/planning` 3 future-only inline, Visit Management **none**). All now read one `date-range-presets.ts` beside the primitive — `pastDateRangePresets` / `upcomingDateRangePresets` / `windowDateRangePresets`, labels once in `common.scope` (en+ar). `/planning` keeps its forward presets via the `window` set: it filters a span that crosses today, so dropping them for "consistency" would have removed the planner's main filter. **The common header's picker was broken:** `ShellScopeControls` required 16 string keys and got 8, and `locale` was never passed — five of seven presets rendered `undefined` labels and the topbar never showed Arabic-Indic digits. That is the `shell-topbar.tsx:81` error carried as "pre-existing" for two sessions; it was masking two live defects. **`npm run typecheck` is now clean across the whole repo — first time on this branch.** Also renamed two presets that lied: `yesterday` was a 2-day span *ending today*, and `thisMonth/Quarter/Year` were plain 30/90/365-day spans, not calendar periods the primitive can express. **Visit status** is now a pinging `StatusPill` in the table and spine (was bare text beside a pill), via a `VISIT_OPERATIONAL_TONE` map over the governed `operational_state` enum read from the migrations — tones are presentation, unknown values fall back to `neutral`. **PingDot:** `aspect-ratio: 1` makes it circular even if a flex/grid parent stretches it (the explicit sizes were never the guarantee); `vertical-align: middle` removes the inline-baseline offset that read as off-centre; `transform-origin: center` and `will-change` stated so the expansion stays concentric and compositor-only. Zero design-system findings, i18n parity held (common 39, planning 261 — two orphaned preset keys deleted). **Static verification only. Parked: two pinging pills per row × 100 rows wants a motion look.** |
| 2026-08-09 | T-021c · Primitive refinements + Visit Management skeleton | [2026-08-09-T-021c-primitive-refinements-and-visits-skeleton](sessions/2026-08/2026-08-09-T-021c-primitive-refinements-and-visits-skeleton.md) | Four owner-reported defects, three fixed in **base primitives** so every screen inherits them. **`DataTable`:** `[data-width="grow"] { inline-size: 100% }` handed all surplus width to one column — the reported dead gap after FACTORY *and* the `V-2062` wrap were the same bug. Rule deleted and the **`grow` rung removed from `DataColumn.width`** (now `"min" \| "auto"`), following the T-030 "delete the rung" precedent; auto layout now distributes slack across all columns. 20 call sites across dashboard/factories/operations/planning/visits updated, `grep grow` returns zero. **`Select`/`MenuRow`:** the count was a `CountBadge` *sibling* of the label — it now rides **inside** it, and the selected-check moved from the leading edge to the **end**, killing the dead gutter at the start of every unselected row. **Corrected after owner review:** the first cut used a bare `<sup>` and lost the badge's rounded-square surface; the chrome was never the problem, only the scale and placement. The superscript is now a **variant of `CountBadge` itself** (`superscript`) reusing the *same* surface/tone declarations — light and dark identical by construction, no second copy to drift — with only the box, corner and type role scaled down. The two duplicated `.count` rules are deleted; `CountBadge` goes from one consumer to three. **`StatusPill`:** `[data-ping]` overrode only `padding-inline-start`, so every pinging pill had double the air at the leading edge — one symmetric `--sqx-space-3` for all pills now, `[data-ping]` adjusts only `gap`. **Skeleton:** the legacy centred "Loading visits" `EmptyState` is replaced by `visits-skeleton`, mirroring the real composition section for section (scope bar → 5 status tiles → filter bar → spine → shaded-header table → footer) on existing primitives, matching the dashboard/factories pattern; `/planning/visits/loading.tsx` was a re-export that highlighted `/visits` in the rail and is now its own file. Typecheck clean, zero design-system findings, 263 i18n keys at parity. **Static verification only** — all four are visual and want a browser pass in both themes and RTL. **Parked for an owner ruling: WEB-000 §2 bans `/* */` but the design-system CSS files all carry rationale comments; I followed the files' convention.** |
